\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\,e\big[x_{i}^{[N]}(s)-y_{i}^{[N]}(s)\big]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\big(x_{i}^{[N]}(t),y_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\,g(x_{i}^{[N]}(s))\,\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}\big(x_{i}^{[N]}(t),y_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big).

Therefore

(9.120)

\displaystyle M^{[N],f}(t,\omega)

\displaystyle=f\big(x_{i}^{[N]}(t,\omega),y_{i}^{[N]}(t,\omega)\big)-f\big(x_{i}^{[N]}(0,\omega),y_{i}^{[N]}(0,\omega)\big)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\,G_{\dagger}^{[N]}\big(f(x_{i}^{[N]}(s,\omega),y_{i}^{[N]}(s,\omega)),s,\omega\big)

is a square-integrable martingale on
\left(\Omega,(\mathcal{F}_{s})_{s\geq 0},\mathbb{P}\right)
.

To check that the sequence of component processes
((x_{i}^{[N]}(t),y_{i}^{[N]}(t)))_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight, we need the local characteristics of the
\mathcal{D}
-semi-martingale, which are defined in
[
JM86
, Definition 3.1.2]
as (recall (
9.118
))

(9.121)

\displaystyle b^{[N]}_{1}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=G^{[N]}_{\dagger}(f_{1},(x,y),t,\omega),

\displaystyle b^{[N]}_{2}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=G^{[N]}_{\dagger}(f_{2},(x,y),t,\omega),

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(1,1)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=G^{[N]}_{\dagger}(f_{1}f_{1},(x,y),t,\omega)-2x\,b_{1}((x,y),t,\omega),

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(2,1)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=G^{[N]}_{\dagger}(f_{1}f_{2},(x,y),t,\omega)-x\,b_{2}((x,y),t,\omega)-yb_{1}((x,y),t,\omega),

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(1,2)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=a_{(2,1)}((x,y),t,\omega),

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(2,2)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=G^{[N]}_{\dagger}(f_{2}f_{2},(x,y),t,\omega)-2y\,b_{2}((x,y),t,\omega).

Hence

(9.122)

\displaystyle b^{[N]}_{1}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}[x_{j}^{[N]}(t,\omega)-x]+Ke[y-x],

\displaystyle b^{[N]}_{2}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=e[x-y],

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(1,1)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=2g(x),

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(1,2)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=a_{(2,1)}((x,y),t,\omega)=0,

\displaystyle a^{[N]}_{(2,2)}((x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=0.

Here,
b^{[N]}_{i}
and
a^{[N]}_{i,j}
,
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, are called the local coefficients of first and second order. We check that the hypotheses
[
JM86
, H1, H2, H3 in Section 3.2.1]
are satisfied.

H_{1}
:

For all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(9.123)

\sum_{i\in\{1,2\}}|b^{[N]}_{i}((x,y),t,\omega)|^{2}+\sum_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}|a^{[N]}_{(i,j)}((x,y),t,\omega)|^{2}\leq 4(c+Ke+e)^{2}+2||g||^{2}.

Hence, choosing as positive adapted process the constant process
1
and letting the constant be equal to
4(c+Ke+e)^{2}+2||g||^{2}
, we see that
H_{1}
is satisfied.

H_{2}
:

Since the component processes are bounded by
1
, also
H_{2}
is satisfied.

H_{3}
:

Since the increasing càdlàg function
(A^{[N]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
in
[
JM86
, Definition 3.1.1]
is in our case
A^{[N]}(t)=t
, also
H_{3}
is satisfied.

Since
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
,
H_{3}
are met,
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
implies that
((x_{i}^{[N]}(t),y_{i}^{[N]}(t))_{t>0})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight in the space of càdlàg paths
\mathcal{D}((0,\infty],[0,1]^{2})
. Hence (
9.116
) indeed holds.
∎

10
Preparation:
N\to\infty
, two-colour mean-field

In this section we extend the results obtained in Section
8.1
to a mean-field system where the seed-bank consists of two colours, one colour that interacts on the slow time scale and one colour that interacts on the fast time scale. To do so we follow the set-up used in Chapter
4
. In particular, we highlight the role of the second colour. Section
10.1
builds up the setting and states the main scaling result: Proposition
10.2
. Section
10.2
provides the proof of this proposition based on a series of lemmas, which are stated and proved first.

10.1
Two-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme

Setup.

In this section we consider a simplified version of our SSDE in (
2.20
) on the finite geographic space

(10.1)

[N]=\{0,1,\ldots,N-1\},\qquad N\in\mathbb{N}.

The migration kernel
a^{\Omega_{N}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is replaced by the migration kernel
a^{[N]}(i,j)=c_{0}N^{-1}
for all
i,j\in[N]
, where
c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
is a constant. The seed-bank consists of
two colours
, labeled
0
and
1
. The exchange rates between the active and the colour-
0
dormant population are given by
K_{0}e_{0},e_{0}
. The exchange rates between active and the colour-
1
dormant population are given by
\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N},\frac{e_{1}}{N}
. The state space is

(10.2)

S=\mathfrak{s}^{[N]},\qquad\mathfrak{s}=[0,1]\times[0,1]^{2},

and the system, consisting of three components, is denoted by

(10.3)

\displaystyle Z^{[N]}(t)=\big(X^{[N]}(t),(Y_{0}^{[N]}(t),Y_{1}^{[N]}(t))\big)_{t\geq 0},

\displaystyle\big(X^{[N]}(t),(Y_{0}^{[N]}(t),Y_{1}^{[N]}(t))\big)=\big(x_{i}(t),(y_{i,0}(t),y_{i,1}(t))\big)_{i\in[N]}.

The components of
(Z^{[N]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
evolve according to the SSDE

(10.4)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N]}_{i}(t)=\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}[x^{[N]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x^{[N]}_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,[y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N],

which is a special case of (
2.20
). The initial state is
\mu(0)=\mu^{\otimes[N]}
for some
\mu\in\mathcal{P}([0,1]^{3})
. The SSDE in (
10.4
) has a unique weak solution coming from a well-posed martingale problem
[
SS80
, Theorem 3.1]
. By
[
SS80
, Theorem 3.2]
, (
10.4
) has a unique strong solution for every deterministic initial state
Z^{[N]}(0)
. Therefore the solutino of (
10.4
) is Feller and Markov for any initial law. The SSDE in (
10.4
) can alternatively be written as

(10.5)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N]}_{i}(t)=c_{0}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(t))\big)}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,[y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N].

So the migration term for a single colony can be interpreted as a drift towards the average of the active population. We are interested in
\mathcal{L}[(Z^{[N]}(t)))_{t\geq 0}]
in the limit as
N\to\infty
, on time scales
t
and
Ns
. Heuristically, analysing the SSDE in (
10.5
), we can foresee the following results, which are made precise in Proposition
10.2
.

\bullet

On time scale
1=N^{0}
(space-time scale
0
), in the limit as
N\to\infty
the colour-
1
dormant population
(Y^{[N]}_{1}(t))_{t\geq 0}
in (
10.4
) converges to a constant process, since the single components
y_{i,1}
do not move on time scale
t
. The components of
(X^{[N]}(t),Y_{0}^{[N]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
converge to i.i.d. copies of the single-colony McKean-Vlasov process in (
7.1
), where in the corresponding SSDE the parameters
c,e,K
are replaced by
c_{0},e_{0},K_{0}
and
E=1
. So on time scale
t
we only see the colour-
0
dormant population interacting with the active population, and the colour-
1
dormant population is not yet coming into play. Therefore the colour-
0
dormant population is the
effective seed-bank
on time scale
1
, and the process

(10.6)

z_{0}^{\mathrm{eff},[N]}(t)=(x^{[N]}_{0}(t),y^{[N]}_{0,0}(t))_{t\geq 0}

is called the effective process on level
0
. Note that the active population has a drift towards
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}x_{j}(t)
, which in the McKean-Vlasov limit is replaced by
\mathbb{E}[x(t)]
given by (
4.51
).

\bullet

On time scale
N
(space-time scale
1
), we look at the averages

(10.7)

\displaystyle(z_{1}^{[N]}(s))_{s>0}

\displaystyle=\left(x_{1}^{[N]}(s),(y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s),y_{1,1}^{[N]}(s))\right)_{s>0}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns),\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(Ns),\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N]}_{i,1}(Ns)\right)\right)_{s>0}.

Again the lower index
1
indicates that the average is the analogue of the 1-block average defined in (
2.22
). Using (
10.4
), we see that the dynamics of the system in (
10.7
) is given by the SSDE

(10.8)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{1}^{[N]}(s)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns))}\,{\rm d}w(s)+NK_{0}e_{0}\left[y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)-x_{1}^{[N]}(s)\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\left[y_{1,1}^{[N]}(s)-x_{1}^{[N]}(s)\right]{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)

\displaystyle=Ne_{0}\left[x_{1}^{[N]}(s)-y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)\right]{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,1}^{[N]}(s)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[x_{1}^{[N]}(s)-y_{1,1}^{[N]}(s)\right]{\rm d}s.

Thus, as in the mean-field system with one-colour, on time scale
N
infinite rates appear in the interaction of the active population with the colour-
0
dormant population. Therefore in the limit as
N\to\infty
the path becomes rougher and rougher at rarer and rarer times. Using the
Meyer-Zheng topology
we can prove that
\lim_{N\to\infty}y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)=\lim_{N\to\infty}x_{1}^{[N]}(s)
most of the time. On the other hand, on time scale
N
,
x_{1}^{[N]}(s)
has a non-trivial interaction with
y_{1,1}^{[N]}(s)
, and therefore we say that on time scale
N
the colour-
1
dormant population is the
effective seed-bank
. Note that for the evolution of the average
\frac{x_{1}^{[N]}(s)+K_{0}y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)}{1+K_{0}}
the rates with a factor
N
in front cancel out. We will use the quantity
\frac{x_{1}^{[N]}(s)+K_{0}y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)}{1+K_{0}}
to obtain results in the classical path-space topology. We call

(10.9)

(z_{1}^{[N],\mathrm{eff}}(s))_{s>0}=\left(\frac{x_{1}^{[N]}(s)+K_{0}y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)}{1+K_{0}},y_{1,1}(s)\right)_{s>0}

the effective process on space-time scale 1. We will call space-time scale
1
also level
1
.

Scaling limit.

To describe the limiting dynamics of the system in (
10.4
), we need the infinite-dimensional process

(10.10)

(Z(t))_{t\geq 0}=\big((z_{i}(t))_{t\geq 0}\big)_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=\big((x_{i}(t),(y_{i,0}(t),y_{i,1}(t)))_{t\geq 0}\big)_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}

with state space
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
that evolves according to

(10.11)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=c_{0}[\theta-x_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y_{i,0}(t)-x_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{i,0}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{0}\,[x_{i}(t)-y_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y_{i,1}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{i,1},\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Here,
\theta\in[0,1]
and
y_{i,1}\in[0,1]
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We will also need the limiting effective process

(10.12)

(Z^{\mathrm{eff}}(t))_{t\geq 0}=\big((z^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t))_{t\geq 0}\big)_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=\big((x_{i}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t),y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i,0}(t))_{t\geq 0}\big)_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}

with state space
([0,1]^{2})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
that evolves according to

(10.13)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=c_{0}[\theta-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g\big(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t)\big)}\,{\rm d}w(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i,0}(t)-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i,0}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{0}\,[x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i}(t)-y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Like for the one-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme, we need the following list of ingredients to formally state our multi-scaling properties:

1.

For positive times
t>0
, we define the so-called
estimators
for the finite system by:

(10.14)

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N]}_{i}(t)+K_{0}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)}{1+K_{0}},

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t).

We abbreviate

(10.15)

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left({\Theta}_{x}^{(1),[N]}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(t)\right),

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(t)\right).

We refer to
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
as the
effective estimator process
and to
({\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
as the
estimator process
.

2.

The
time scale

Ns
is such that
\mathcal{L}[\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))-\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns)]=\delta_{0}
for all
L(N)
satisfying
\lim_{N\to\infty}

L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, but not for
L(N)=N
. In words,
Ns
is the time scale on which
\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(\cdot)
starts evolving, i.e.,
(\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}
is no longer a fixed process. When we scale time by
Ns
, we will use
s
as a time index, which indicates the “fast time scale”. The “slow time scale” will be indicated by
t
. Thus, the time scales for the two-colour mean-field system are the same as the time scales for the one-colour mean-field system.

Remark 10.1
.

[Notation]

The upper index
1
in
\bar{\Theta}^{(1)}
and
\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1)}
is used to indicate that we are working with a system of level 1, so the system that lives on space-time scale 1. This can later be easily generalized to levels
2
and
k
.

\blacksquare

3.

The
invariant measure
(i.e., the equilibrium measure) for the evolution of a single colony in (
10.11
), written

(10.16)

\Gamma_{\theta,\theta,y_{1}},

and the
invariant measure
of the infinite system in (
10.11
), written
\nu_{\theta,\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}=\Gamma_{\theta,\theta,y_{1}}^{\otimes\mathbb{N}_{0}}
with
\theta\in[0,1]
and
{\bf y}_{1}\in[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
a random variable. The existence of the invariant measure
\nu_{\theta}
and the convergence of
\mathcal{L}[Z(t)_{t\geq 0}]
towards
\nu_{\theta}
will be shown in the proof of Proposition
10.2
.

4.

The invariant measure of the effective process in (
10.13
),

(10.17)

\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta},

and the invariant measure for the full process,
\nu^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta}=(\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta})^{\otimes\mathbb{N}_{0}}
.

5.

The renormalisation transformation
\mathcal{F}\colon\,\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}
,

(10.18)

(\mathcal{F}g)(\theta)=\int_{([0,1]^{2})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}}g(x_{0})\,\nu^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta}({\rm d}x_{0},{\rm d}y_{0,0}),\quad\theta\in[0,1],

where
\Gamma_{\theta}^{\mathrm{eff}}
is the equilibrium measure of (
10.16
). Note that this is the same transformation as defined in (
4.16
), but for the truncated system. Since
\nu^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta}
is a product measure, we can write

(10.19)

(\mathcal{F}g)(\theta)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}g(x)\,\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\theta}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}y_{0}),\quad\theta\in[0,1],

6.

The limiting 1-block process
(z_{1}(s))_{s>0}=(x_{1}(s),(y_{0,1}(s),y_{1,1}(s)))_{s>0}
evolving according to

(10.20)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\left[\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}g)\big(x_{1}(s)\big)}\,{\rm d}w(s)+K_{1}e_{1}\left[y_{1,1}(s)-x_{1}(s)\right]{\rm d}s\right],

\displaystyle y_{0,1}(s)

\displaystyle=x_{1}(s),

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,1}(s)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[x_{1}(s)-y_{1,1}(s)\right]\,{\rm d}s,

where
\mathcal{F}g
is defined in (
10.20
). The effective process
(z^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s))_{s>0}=(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s),y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(s))_{s>0}
on space-time scale
1
,

(10.21)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\left[\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}g)(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s))}\,{\rm d}w(s)+K_{1}e_{1}\,[y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(s)-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s)]\,{\rm d}s\right],

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(s)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\,[x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s)-y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(s)]\,{\rm d}s.

We are now ready to state the scaling limit for the evolution of the averages in (
10.7
), which we refer to as the
mean-field finite-systems scheme with two colours
.

Proposition 10.2
.

[Mean-field: two-colour finite-systems scheme]

Suppose that
\mathcal{L}[Z^{[N]}(0)]=\mu^{\otimes[N]}
for some
\mu\in\mathcal{P}\left([0,1]\times[0,1]^{2}\right)
. Let

(10.22)

\vartheta_{0}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[\frac{x+K_{0}y_{0}}{1+K_{0}}\right],\qquad\qquad\theta_{y_{1}}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[y_{1}\right].

(a)

For the effective estimator process defined in (
10.15
),

(10.23)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\right)_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right],

where the limit is determined by the unique solution of the SSDE (
10.21
), with initial state

(10.24)

\displaystyle z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0)=\left(x_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0),y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(0)\right)=\left(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}\right).

(b)

Assume for the
1
-dormant single components that

(10.25)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns)\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)\right]=P^{z_{1}(s)}_{Y_{1}(s)}.

Define

(10.26)

\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})}(s)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}S_{s}\bigl((\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}),{\rm d}(u_{x},u_{y})\bigr)\,\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{u_{x}}\in\mathcal{P}([0,1]^{2}),

where
S_{s}((\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}),\cdot)
is the time-
s
marginal law of the process
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s))_{s>0}
starting from
(\theta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})\in[0,1]^{2}
and
\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{u_{x}}
is the equilibrium distribution of the system in (
10.13
) with
\theta=u_{x}
(note that
\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}}(0)=\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\vartheta_{0}}
). Let
(z^{\mathrm{eff},\Gamma_{(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})}(s)}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be the process with initial law
z^{\mathrm{eff},\Gamma_{(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})}(s)}(0)
drawn according to
\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})}(s)
(which is a mixture of random processes in equilibrium) that, conditional on
x_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s)=\theta
, evolves according to (
10.13
). Then, for every
s\in(0,\infty)
,

(10.27)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z^{\mathrm{eff},[N]}_{0}(Ns+t)\right)_{t\geq 0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z^{\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff}}_{(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})}(s)}(t))_{t\geq 0}\right].

(c)

For the averages in (
10.7
),

(10.28)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}^{[N]}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right]

\displaystyle\text{in the Meyer-Zheng topology},

where the limit process is the unique solution of the SSDE in (
10.20
) with initial state

(10.29)

z_{1}(0)=\left(x_{1}(0),y_{0,1}(0),y_{1,1}(0)\right)=\left(\vartheta_{0},\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}\right).

(d)

Assume
10.25
and define

(10.30)

\nu(s)=\int_{[0,1]^{3}}S_{s}\bigl((\vartheta_{0},\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}),{\rm d}(u_{x},u_{x},u_{y_{1}})\bigr)\,\int_{[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}}P^{(u_{x},u_{x},u_{y_{1}})}_{Y_{1}(s)}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}})\,\nu_{u_{x},{\bf y_{1}}},

where
S_{s}((\vartheta_{0},\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}),\cdot)
is the time-
s
marginal law of the process
\left(z_{1}(s)\right)_{s>0}
in (
10.20
), starting from
(\vartheta_{0},\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}})\in[0,1]^{3}
, and
\nu_{u_{x},{\bf y_{1}}}
is the equilibrium distribution of the system in (
10.11
) with
\theta=u_{x}
and
(y_{i,1})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=\bf{y_{1}}
, (note that
\nu(0)=\nu_{\vartheta_{0},(y_{i,1}(0))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}}
). Let
(z^{\nu(s)}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be the process on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
with initial measure
z^{\nu(s)}(0)
drawn according to
\nu(s)
(which is a mixture of random processes in equilibrium) that conditional on
x_{1}(s)=\theta
and
Y_{1}(s)={\bf y_{1}}
evolves according to (
10.11
) with
\theta=u_{x}
and
(y_{i,1})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}={\bf y_{1}}
. Then, for every
s\in(0,\infty)
,

(10.31)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(Z^{[N]}(Ns+t)\right)_{t\geq 0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z^{\nu(s)}(t))_{t\geq 0}\right].

Remark 10.3

(
Law of
1
-dormant single components).

Note that

(10.32)

\left(\mathcal{L}\left[Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns)\Big|\left(\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns),\Theta_{y_{1}}^{[N]}(Ns)\right)\right]\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}

is a tight sequence of measures. Hence there exist weak limit points. In Section
11
we will see that if there is a higher layer in the hierarchy, then we can show that all weak limit points of (
10.32
) are the same and we can identify the limit. For Theorems
4.2
and
4.4
we do not need this assumption, since there will alwyas be multiple higher levels.

\blacksquare

10.2
Proof of the two-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme

The proof of Proposition
10.2
, the finite-systems scheme with one level and two colours, follows the strategy used in Section
9
for the proof of Proposition
8.1
. Like for the one-colour finite-systems scheme, we denote the slow time scale by
t
and the fast time scale by
s
. The proof consists of the following 6 steps:

1.

Tightness of the effective estimator processes defined in (
10.15
).

(10.33)

\big(({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}\big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

2.

Stability property of
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns+t))_{t>0}
, i.e., for
L(N)
satisfying
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, and all
\epsilon>0
,

(10.34)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\right|>\epsilon\right]=0.

and

(10.35)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\right|>\epsilon\right]=0.

3.

Equilibrium of the infinite system and the one-dimensional distribution of the effective single components
(Z(Ns+t))_{t>0}
, analogous to Proposition
8.4
.

4.

Limiting evolution of the effective processes
(({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
.

5.

Evolution of the
1
-blocks in the Meyer-Zheng topology.

6.

Proof of Proposition
10.2
.

Step 1: Tightness of the 1-block estimators.

Lemma 10.4
.

[Tightness of the 1-block estimator]

Let

(10.36)

({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}

be defined as in (
10.14
). Then
(\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}])_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a tight sequence of probability measures on
\mathcal{C}((0,\infty),[0,1]^{2})
.

Proof.

To prove tightness of
(({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, we will prove for all
\epsilon>0
that the set of measures
(({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s\geq\epsilon})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. To do so, fix
\epsilon>0
. We will again use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
. From (
10.4
) we find that the 1-block averages
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}
evolve according to

(10.37)

\displaystyle{\rm d}\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg[\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns)\big)}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(s)

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\left[\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns)\right]\,{\rm d}s\Bigg],

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)\right]\,{\rm d}s.

To use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
, we define
\mathcal{C}^{*}
as the set of polynomials on
([0,1]^{2})
. Note that
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s\geq\epsilon}
is a semi-martingale. Applying Itô’s formula, we get

(10.38)

\displaystyle f\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\right)

\displaystyle=f\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(N\epsilon)\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon}^{s}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(Nr)\big)}\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr)\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon}^{s}{\rm d}r\,\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\left[\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Nr)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Nr)\right]\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr)\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon}^{s}{\rm d}r\,e_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Nr)-\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Nr)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr)\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon}^{s}{\rm d}r\,\frac{1}{2(1+K_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(Nr)\big)\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr)\right)

for all
f\in\mathcal{C}^{*}
. Hence, if we define the operator

(10.39)

\displaystyle G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}

\displaystyle\colon\,(\mathcal{C}^{*},[0,1]^{2},[\epsilon,\infty),\Omega)\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}(f,(x,y),s,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\left[y-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns,\omega)\right]\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}

\displaystyle+e_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns,\omega)-y\right]\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2(1+K_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns,\omega))\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}},

then we see that the process
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s\geq\epsilon}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-semi-martingale for all
\epsilon>0
. For all
\epsilon>0
the conditions
H_{1},\ H_{2},\ H_{3}
are satisfied as before. Therefore we conclude from
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
that the sequence
(({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s\geq\epsilon})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. Since this is true for all
\epsilon>0
, we conclude that
(\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}])_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight.
∎

Step 2: Stability of the
1
-block estimators.

Lemma 10.5
.

[Stability property of the 1-block estimator]

Let
{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(t)
be defined as in (
10.14
). For any
L(N)
satisfying
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
,

(10.40)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\right|=0\text{ in probability}

and

(10.41)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\right|=0\text{ in probability. }

Proof.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. From the SSDE (
10.4
) we obtain that, for
N
large enough,

(10.42)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}r\,\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\left[\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(r)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N]}_{i}(r)\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(r)\big)}\,\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Bigg|\frac{L(N)2K_{1}e_{1}}{N(1+K_{0})}\Bigg|+\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\,\Bigg|\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(r)\big)}\,\Bigg|>\epsilon-\frac{L(N)2K_{1}e_{1}}{N(1+K_{0})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(r)\big)}\,\Bigg|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Bigg).

Applying the same optional stopping argument as used in the proof of Lemma
8.15
, we find (
10.40
). For (
10.41
), note that

(10.43)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\int_{Ns-t}^{Ns}{\rm d}r\,\frac{e_{1}}{N}\left[\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(r)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x_{i}^{[N]}(r)\right]\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{2e_{1}L(N)}{(1+K_{0})N}>\epsilon\Bigg).

Let
N\to\infty
to obtain (
10.41
).
∎

Step 3: Equilibrium for the infinite system.

To derive the equilibrium of the single components in the infinite system, we derive the following analoque of Proposition
8.4
. Recall that the finite system is denoted by
Z^{[N_{k}]}
in (
10.3
), and recall the list of ingredients in Section
10.1
.

Proposition 10.6
.

[Equilibrium for the infinite 2-colour system]

Let
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
\mathbb{N}
. Fix
s>0
. Let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, and suppose that

(10.44)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\right]=P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[Y_{1}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\right]=P^{{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1)}(s)}_{Y_{1}(s)},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N_{k})}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)-\bar{\Theta}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-t)\right|+\left|{\Theta}_{y_{1}}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)-{\Theta_{y_{1}}}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\delta_{0},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\bigr]=\nu(s).

Then
\nu(s)
is of the form

(10.45)

\nu(s)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}({\rm d}\theta,{\rm d}\theta_{y})\,\int_{[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}}P^{(\theta,\theta_{y})}_{Y_{1}(s)}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}})\,\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}},

where
{\bf y_{1}}=(y_{i,1})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is a sequence with elements in
[0,1]
, and
\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}
is the equilibrium measure of the process in (
10.10
) evolving according to (
10.11
) with
(y_{i,1})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
given by the sequence
{\bf y_{1}}=
.

Preparation for the proof of Proposition
10.6
.

The proof of Proposition
10.6
follows the same line of argument as used in the proof of Proposition
8.4
. We need lemmas that are similar to Lemmas
8.5
-
8.11
, but this time in the setting of the two-colour hierarchical mean-field finite-systems scheme. Afterwards we prove Proposition
10.6
.

Lemma 10.7
.

[Convergence for the infinite system]

Let
\mu
be an exchangeable probability measure on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Then for the system
(Z(t))_{t\geq 0}
given by (
10.10
) with
\mathcal{L}[Z(0)]=\mu
,

(10.46)

\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[Z(t)]=\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}},

where
\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}
is of the form

(10.47)

\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Gamma_{\theta,y_{i,1}}

with
\Gamma_{\theta,y_{i,1}}
the equilibrium of the
i
th single-component process in (
10.11
).

Proof.

For each component of the infinite system in (
10.10
) the
1
-dormant single component process
(y_{i,1}(t))_{t\geq 0}
does not move on time scale
t
. Hence, given the states of
1
-dormant single components, we can use a similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
7.2
(see Section
7.3
) to show that the single components converge to an equilibrium measure
\Gamma_{\theta,y_{i,1}}
. Since the single components do not interact, the claim in Lemma
10.7
follows.
∎

The second lemma establishes the continuity of the equilibrium with respect to
\theta
, its center of drift.

Lemma 10.8
.

[Continuity of the equilibrium]

Let
\mathcal{P}(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
denote the space of probability measures on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. The mapping
[0,1]\times[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\to\mathcal{P}(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
given by

(10.48)

(\theta,{\bf y_{1}})\mapsto\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}

is continuous. Furthermore, if
h
is a Lipschitz function on
[0,1]
, then also
\mathcal{F}h
defined by

(10.49)

(\mathcal{F}h)(\theta)=\mathbb{E}^{\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}}[h(\cdot)]=\int_{([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}}\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{1}}}({\rm d}z)\,h(x_{0})

is a Lipschitz function on
[0,1]
, whose values are independent of
{\bf y_{1}}
.

Proof.

Lemma
10.8
follows from the proof of Lemma
10.12
.
∎

The third lemma characterises the speed at which the estimators
({\Theta}^{[N]}_{x}(t))_{t\geq 0}
and
({\Theta}^{[N]}_{y}(t))_{t\geq 0}
converge to each other when
N\to\infty
and
t\to\infty
.

Lemma 10.9
.

[Comparison of empirical averages]

Let
(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be defined as in (
10.14
). Then

(10.50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(0)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(0)\right)^{2}\right]}{\rm e}^{-(K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0})t}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{2}{K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0}}\left[\frac{||g||}{N}+\frac{4K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\right]}.

Proof.

From (
10.4
) it follows via Itô-calculus that

(10.51)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-2(K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0})\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{[N]}(t),

where

(10.52)

\displaystyle h^{[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right)\left[\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N]}\mathbb{E}\big[g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(r)\big)\big].

Hence

(10.53)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(0)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(0)\right)^{2}\right]{\rm e}^{-2(K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0})t}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,{\rm e}^{-2(K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0})(t-r)}h^{[N]}(r).

Take the square root on both sides and use Jensen’s inequality to get (
10.50
).
∎

Like for the mean-field system with one colour, we need to compare the finite system in (
10.3
) with an infinite system. To derive the analogue of Lemma
8.9
, let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
. Define the measure
\mu_{N}
on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
by continuing the configuration of

(10.54)

Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))=\left(X^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),\left(Y_{0}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))\right)\right)

periodically to
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Let

(10.55)

\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N]}_{i}(Ns-L(N))+K_{0}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(Ns-L(N))}{1+K_{0}}.

Let

(10.56)

(Z^{\mu_{N}}(t))_{t\geq 0}=\bigl(X^{\mu_{N}}(t),\left(Y_{0}^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{N}}(t)\right)\bigr)_{t\geq 0}

be the infinite system evolving according to

(10.57)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=c_{0}\,[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}-x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g\big(x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)\big)}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)-x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{0}\,[x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)-y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(0),\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

starting from initial distribution
\mu_{N}
. Then the following lemma is the equivalent of Lemma
8.9
for the two-colour mean-field system.

Lemma 10.10
.

[Comparison of finite and infinite systems]

Fix
s>0
, and let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N
. Suppose that

(10.58)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns-t)\right|=0\ \text{ in probability}.

Then, for all
t\geq 0
,

(10.59)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f\big(Z^{\mu_{N}}(t)\big)-f\big(Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)+t)\big)\right]\right|=0\qquad\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C}\bigl(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mathbb{R}\bigr).

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
8.9
. We rewrite the SSDE in (
10.4
) as

(10.60)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N]}_{i}(t)=c_{0}\,\big[\Theta^{(1),[N]}-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)\big]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+c_{0}\,\big[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}\big]\,{\rm d}t+c_{0}\,\big[\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)\big]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\sqrt{g\big(x^{[N]}_{i}(t)\big)}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,\big[y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N]}_{i}(t)\big]{\rm d}t+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\big[y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)-x_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big]{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,\big[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)\big]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,\big[x^{[N]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)\big]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N].

As before, we consider the finite system in (
10.60
) as a system on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
by periodic continuation, and we couple the finite system in (
10.60
) and the infinite system in (
10.59
) via there Brownian motions. We denote the coupled process by
\tilde{z}(t)=(\tilde{z}_{i}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=(\tilde{z}^{[N]}_{i}(t),\tilde{z}^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, where
\tilde{z}^{[N]}_{i}(t)=(\tilde{x}^{[N]}_{i}(t),\tilde{y}^{[N]}_{i,0}(t),\tilde{y}^{[N]}_{i,1}(t))
and
\tilde{z}^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)=(\tilde{x}^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t),\tilde{y}^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t),\tilde{y}^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(t))
. We define

(10.61)

\displaystyle\Delta_{i,0}^{[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{x}_{i}^{[N]}(t)-\tilde{x}_{i}^{\mu_{N}}(t),

\displaystyle\delta_{i,0}^{[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{y}_{i,0}^{[N]}(t)-\tilde{y}_{i,0}^{\mu_{N}}(t),

\displaystyle\delta_{i,1}^{[N]}(t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{y}_{i,1}^{[N]}(t)-\tilde{y}_{i,1}^{\mu_{N}}(t).

As in the proof of Lemma
8.9
, we have to show that, for all
t\geq 0
,

(10.62)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta^{[N]}_{i}(t)|]=0,\qquad\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)|]=0,\qquad\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\delta^{[N]}_{i,1}(t)|]=0.

To prove the third limit in (
10.63
), note that, by (
10.57
), (
10.60
) and the choice of the initial measure in the coupling,

(10.63)

y_{i,1}^{[N]}(t)=y_{i,1}^{[N]}(0)+\frac{e_{1}}{N}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,\big[x_{i}^{[N]}(r)-y_{i,1}^{[N]}(r)\big]=y_{i,1}^{\mu_{N}}(t)+\frac{e_{1}}{N}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,\big[x_{i}^{[N]}(r)-y_{i,1}^{[N]}(r)\big].

Hence

(10.64)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\delta^{[N]}_{i,1}(L(N))|]=0.

To prove the first two limits in (
10.63
), we argue as in the proof of Lemma
8.9
, but we need to add extra drift terms towards the first seed-bank. Using Itô-calculus, we obtain

(10.65)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)|+K|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)|]

\displaystyle=-c\,\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)]

\displaystyle-2K_{0}e_{0}\,\mathbb{E}\left[[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)|+|\delta^{[N]}_{i}(t)|]\,1_{\{{\rm sign}\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\neq{\rm sign}\delta_{i,0}^{[N]}(t)\}}\right]

\displaystyle+c\,{\rm sign}\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}\big]

\displaystyle+c\,{\rm sign}\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big[\bar{\Theta}_{x}^{(1),[N]}(t)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(t)\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,{\rm sign}\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big[\delta_{i,1}^{[N]}(t)-\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\big].

This can be rewritten as

(10.66)

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)|+K_{0}|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)|]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(0)|+K|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(0)|]-c\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(r)]

\displaystyle-2K_{0}e_{0}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,\mathbb{E}\left[[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(r)|+|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(r)|]\,1_{\{{\rm sign}\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)\neq{\rm sign}\delta_{i,0}^{[N]}(t)\}}\right]

\displaystyle+c\,\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(r)-{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}|

\displaystyle+c\,\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,|\bar{\Theta}_{x}^{(1),[N]}(r)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(r)|

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}r\,\left|\delta_{i,1}^{[N]}(r)-\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(r)\right|.

By the construction of the measure
\mu_{N}
, we have

(10.67)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(0)|+K_{0}|\delta^{[N]}_{i}(0)|]=0.

Therefore, for all
t\geq 0
,

(10.68)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}^{[N]}(t)|+K_{0}|\delta^{[N]}_{i,0}(t)|]=0.

Combine this with (
10.64
) and use that Lipschitz functions are dense in the set of bounded continuous functions. Then, as in the proof of Lemma
8.9
, we get the claim in (
10.59
).
∎

Before we can prove that the infinite system
(X^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{0}^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{N}}(t))_{t\geq 0}
converges to a limiting system as
N\to\infty
, we need the following regularity property for the estimators
(\bar{\Theta}^{[N]},\Theta_{y_{1}}^{[N]})
.

Lemma 10.11
.

[Stability of the estimator for the conserved quantity]

Define
\mu_{N}
as in Lemma
10.10
. Let
(x^{N}_{i},y^{N}_{i,0},y^{N}_{i,1})_{i\in[N]}
be distributed according to the exchangeable probability measure
\mu_{N}
on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
restricted to
([0,1]^{3})^{[N]}
. Suppose that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{N}=\mu
for some exchangeable probability measure
\mu
on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Define the random variable
\phi
on
(\mu,([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
by putting

(10.69)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x_{i}+Ky_{i,0}}{1+K},\qquad\phi_{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}y_{i,1,}

and the random variable
\phi^{[N]}
on
(\mu_{N},([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
by putting

(10.70)

\displaystyle\phi^{[N]}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{1}^{[N]},\phi_{2}^{[N]})

\displaystyle\phi^{[N]}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{N}_{i}+Ky^{N}_{i,0}}{1+K},\qquad\phi^{[N]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{N}_{i,1}.

Then

(10.71)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[\phi^{[N]}]=\mathcal{L}[\phi].

Proof.

We can use a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.10
. Define

(10.72)

D^{[N]}(Z)=\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}\frac{x_{j}+K_{0}y_{j,0}}{1+K_{0}},\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}y_{i,1}\right).

Then we can proceed as in the proof of Lemma
8.10
, using Fourier analysis for both components of
D^{[N]}(Z)
separately.
∎

In the fifth and final lemma we state the convergence of
\mathcal{L}[(X^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{0}^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{N}}(t))]
to the law of a limiting system as
N\to\infty
.

Lemma 10.12
.

[Uniformity of the ergodic theorem for the infinite system]

Let
\mu_{N}
be defined as in (
10.56
). Since
(\mu_{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight, it has convergent subsequences. Let
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a subsequence such that
\mu=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mu_{N_{k}}
. Define

(10.73)

\Theta=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x_{i}^{\mu}+Ky_{i,0}^{\mu}}{1+K}\,\qquad\text{ in }L_{2}(\mu).

Let
Z^{\mu}(t)=\bigl(X^{\mu}(t),Y_{0}^{\mu}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu}(t)\bigr)_{t\geq 0}
be the infinite system evolving according to

(10.74)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{\mu}_{i}(t)=c\left[\Theta-x^{\mu}_{i}(t)\right]{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x^{\mu}_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)+Ke\,[y^{\mu}_{i,1}(t)-x^{\mu}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{\mu}_{i,0}(t)=e\,[x^{\mu}_{i}(t)-y^{\mu}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{\mu}_{i,1}(t)=y^{\mu}_{i,1}(0),\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

and let
Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(t)=(X^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(t),Y_{0}^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be the infinite system defined in (
10.56
). Then

1.

For all
t\geq 0
,

(10.75)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl[f\bigl(Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(t)\bigr)\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[f\bigl(Z^{\mu}(t)\bigr)\bigr]\bigr|=0\qquad\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C}\bigl(([0,1]^{2})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mathbb{R}\bigr).

2.

There exists a sequence
\bar{L}(N)
satisfying
\lim_{N\to\infty}\bar{L}(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}\bar{L}(N)/N=0
such that

(10.76)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl[f\bigl(Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k})+\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr)-f\bigl(Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr)\bigr|\bigr]

\displaystyle+\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl[f\bigl(Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr)\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[f\bigl(Z^{\mu}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr)\bigr]\bigr|=0\qquad\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C}\bigl(([0,1]^{2})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mathbb{R}\bigr).

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.11
, we can construct
(z_{i}^{\mu_{N}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
and
(z_{i}^{\mu})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
on one probability space. Then

(10.77)

\lim_{N\to\infty}y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(0)=y^{\mu}_{i,1}(0)\text{ a.s. }

and

(10.78)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}-\Theta|]=0.

Via a similar coupling as in Lemma (
10.10
), it follows via Itô-calculus that (
10.75
) holds. Combining (
10.64
), (
10.68
), (
10.77
) and (
10.78
), we obtain, via a similar construction as in the proof of Lemma
8.11
, a sequence
\bar{L}(N)
such that

(10.79)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta^{N}_{i}(\bar{L}(N))|+K_{0}|\delta^{N}_{i,0}(\bar{L}(N))|]+K_{1}|\delta^{N}_{i,1}(\bar{L}(N))|]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[|\Delta^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(\bar{L}(N))|+K_{0}|\delta^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(\bar{L}(N))|]+K_{1}|\delta^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(\bar{L}(N))|]=0.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.11
, we can again use Lipschitz functions to conclude (
10.76
).
∎

Lemma 10.13
.

[Coupling of finite systems]

Let

(10.80)

Z^{[N],1}=(X^{[N],1},Y_{0}^{[N],1},Y_{1}^{[N],1})

be the finite system evolving according to (
10.4
) starting from an exchangeable initial measure. Let
\mu^{[N],1}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N],1}(0)
. Similarly, let

(10.81)

Z^{[N],2}=(X^{[N],2},Y_{0}^{[N],2},Y_{1}^{[N],2})

be the finite system evolving according to (
10.4
) starting from an exchangeable initial measure. Let
\mu^{[N],2}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N],2}(0)
. Let
\tilde{\mu}
be any weak limit point of the sequence of measures
(\mu^{[N],1}\times\mu^{[N],2})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Define the random variables
\bar{\Theta}^{[N],1}
and
\bar{\Theta}^{[N],2}
on
(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\times([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mu^{[N],1}\times\mu^{[N],2})
and
\bar{\Theta}_{1}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{2}
on
(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\times([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\bar{\mu})
by

(10.82)

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{[N],1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N],1}_{i}+K_{0}y^{[N],1}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},\qquad\bar{\Theta}^{[N],2}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N],2}_{i}+K_{0}y^{[N],2}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{1}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x^{1}_{i}+K_{0}y^{1}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},\qquad\bar{\Theta}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x^{2}_{i}+K_{0}y^{2}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},

and let
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N],1}(t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N],2}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be defined as in (
10.14
) for
Z^{[N],1}
, respectively,
Z^{[N],2}
. Suppose that

(10.83)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left(\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N],k}(0)-\bar{\Theta}^{[N],k}(t)\right|\right)=0\ \text{ in probability},\quad k\in\{1,2\},

and suppose that
\tilde{\mu}(\{\bar{\Theta}_{1}=\bar{\Theta}_{2},\,Y^{1}_{1}=Y^{2}_{1}\})=1
. Then, for any
t(N)\to\infty
,

(10.84)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t(N))-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t(N))|+K_{0}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t(N))|

\displaystyle+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t(N))|\bigr]=0.

Proof.

Via standard Itô-calculus we obtain from (
10.4
) that

(10.85)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathbb{E}\left[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t)|+K_{0}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t)|+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t)|\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2c}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]}\mathbb{E}\left[|x^{[N],1}_{j}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{j}(t)|1_{\{{\rm sign}(x^{[N],1}_{j}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{j}(t))\neq{\rm sign}(x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t))\}}\right]

\displaystyle-2K_{0}e_{0}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t)|+K|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t)|

\displaystyle\times 1_{\{{\rm sign}(x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t))\neq{\rm sign}(y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t))\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-2\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t)|+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t)|

\displaystyle\times 1_{\{{\rm sign}(x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t))\neq{\rm sign}(y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t))\}}\bigg].

Therefore, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(10.86)

\displaystyle t\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t)-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t)|+K_{0}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t)|+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t)-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t)|\right]

is a decreasing function. Hence we can use the same strategy as in the proof of Lemma
8.13
to finish the proof.
∎

\bullet
Proof of Proposition
10.6

Proof.

We follow a similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
8.4
. Let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}

L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
. Let
\mu_{N}
be the measure on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
obtained by periodic continuation of
\mathcal{L}[Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))]
. Note that
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is compact. Hence, letting
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be the subsequence in Proposition
10.6
, we can pass to a possibly further subsequence and obtain

(10.87)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mu_{N_{k}}=\mu.

Since we assumed that
\mathcal{L}[Z^{[N]}(0)]
is exchangeable and the dynamics preserve exchangeability, the measures
\mu_{N_{k}}
are translation invariant and also the limiting law
\mu
is translation invariant.

Let
\phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
be defined as in (
10.69
) in Lemma
10.11
. Then we can condition on
\phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
and write

(10.88)

\mu=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\mu_{\rho}\,{\rm d}\Lambda(\rho),

where
\Lambda(\cdot)=\mathcal{L}[\phi]=\mathcal{L}[(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})]
and
\rho=(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
. By assumption we know that

(10.89)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\right]=P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}(\cdot)

and

(10.90)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N_{k})}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)-\bar{\Theta}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-t)\right|+\left|{\Theta}_{y_{1}}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)-{\Theta_{y_{1}}}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\delta_{0}.

Hence

(10.91)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k}))\right]=P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}(\cdot).

Recall that

(10.92)

\Lambda(\cdot)=\mathcal{L}\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x_{i}+Ky_{i,0}}{1+K},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}y_{i,1}\right)\right]\quad\text{ on }(\mu,([0,1]^{2})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}).

By Lemma
8.10
, if

(10.93)

\phi^{N_{k}}=(\phi_{1}^{N_{k}},\phi_{2}^{N_{k}})=\left(\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{i\in[N_{k}]}\frac{x_{i}+Ky_{i,0}}{1+K},\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{i\in[N_{k}]}y^{[N_{k}]}_{i,1}\right)\text{ on }(\mu_{N_{k}},([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}),

then
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[\phi^{N_{k}}]=\mathcal{L}[\phi]
. Taking the subsequence
(\mu_{N_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we get
\Lambda(\cdot)=P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}(\cdot)
, and hence

(10.94)

\mu=\int_{[0,1]}\mu_{\rho}\,{\rm d}P_{s}(\rho).

Let
\bar{L}(N)
be the sequence constructed in Lemma
10.12
[b]. By construction we can require that
\bar{L}(N)\leq L(N)
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Write

(10.95)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k})+\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k})+\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]-\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr],

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu_{N_{k}}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]-\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr].

By Lemma
10.12
the first and second differences tend to zero as
k\to\infty
. Hence

(10.96)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k})+\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]=\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr].

By (
10.88
),

(10.97)

\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]\,P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}({\rm d}\rho).

For the infinite system
(Z^{\mu_{\rho}}(t))_{t\geq 0}=\left(X^{\mu_{\rho}}(t),Y_{0}^{\mu_{\rho}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(t)\right)_{t\geq 0}
we have

(10.98)

Y^{\mu}_{1}(t)=Y^{\mu}_{1}(0)\,a.s.

and hence, since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\bar{L}(N_{k})/N_{k}=0
by (
10.44
),

(10.99)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))]=\mathcal{L}[Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(0)]\,\,\forall\,\rho\in[0,1].

Therefore

(10.100)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))]=P_{Y_{1}(s)}^{\rho}(\cdot)

and

(10.101)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\bigl[X^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),Y_{0}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int\mathcal{L}\bigl[X_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),Y_{0}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),{\bf y_{1}}\bigr]{\rm d}P_{Y_{1}(s)}^{\rho}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}}).

Hence, since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\bar{L}(N_{k})=\infty
, by Lemma
8.5
we have

(10.102)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl[X_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),Y_{0}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k})),Y_{1}^{\mu_{\rho}}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int\nu_{\rho,{\bf y_{1}}}P_{Y_{1}(s)}^{\rho}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}}).

Therefore, by (
9.8
), (
10.97
) and Lemma
8.6
,

(10.103)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k})+\bar{L}(N_{k}))\bigr]=\int_{[0,1]}P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}({\rm d}\rho)\int\nu_{\rho,{\bf y_{1}}}P_{Y_{1}(s)}^{\rho}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}}).

To finish the proof, we proceed as in the proof of Proposition
8.4
and invoke Lemma
10.13
. Let
Z^{[N],1}=(X^{[N],1},Y_{0}^{[N],1},Y_{1}^{[N],1})
be the finite system starting from

(10.104)

\mathcal{L}\bigl[Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))\bigr]=\mathcal{L}\bigl[X^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),Y_{0}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))\bigr].

Let
(\bar{L}(N))_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
be the sequence constructed in Lemma
10.12
. Let
Z^{[N],2}=(X^{[N],2},Y_{0}^{[N],2},Y_{1}^{[N],2})
be the finite system starting from

(10.105)

\mathcal{L}\bigl[X^{[N]}(Ns-\bar{L}(N))\bigr]=\mathcal{L}\left[X^{[N]}(Ns-\bar{L}(N)),Y_{0}^{[N]}(Ns-\bar{L}(N)),Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns-\bar{L}(N))\right].

Choose for
t(N)
in Lemma
10.13
the sequence
\bar{L}(N)
. Let
\mu^{[N],1}
be defined by the periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))
and
\mu^{[N],2}
be defined by periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N]}(Ns-\bar{L}(N))
. Define
\Theta_{1}
and
\Theta_{2}
according to (
8.44
), where under
\mu^{[N],2}
we replace
L(N)
by
\bar{L}(N)
. Then, by the assumptions in (
10.44
),

(10.106)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}|\Theta^{(1),[N_{k}],1}-\Theta^{(1),[N_{k}],2}|

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}|\Theta^{N_{k}}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k}))-\Theta^{N_{k}}(N_{k}s-\bar{L}(N_{k}))|

\displaystyle=0\text{ in probability}.

Using
10.63
we see that also, for all
i\in[N]
,

(10.107)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}|y^{[N_{k}],1}_{i,1}(0)-y^{[N_{k}],2}_{i,1}(0)|

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}|y_{i,1}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-L(N_{k}))-y_{i,1}^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s-\bar{L}(N_{k}))|

\displaystyle=0\text{ in probability}.

Therefore, if
\mu
is any weak limit point of the sequence
\left(\mu^{[N_{k}],1}\times\mu^{[N_{k}],2}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, then

(10.108)

\mu(\{\Theta_{1}=\Theta_{2},\,Y^{1}_{1}=Y^{2}_{1}\})=1.

Hence, by possibly passing to a further subsequence, we can now apply Lemma
10.13
to obtain, for all
i
,

(10.109)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[

\displaystyle|x^{[N_{k}],1}_{i}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))-x^{[N_{k}],2}_{i}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))|

\displaystyle+K_{0}\,|y_{i,0}^{[N_{k}],1}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))-y_{i,0}^{[N_{k}],2}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))|

\displaystyle+K_{1}\,|y_{i,1}^{[N_{k}],1}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))-y_{i,1}^{[N_{k}],2}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))|\Bigr]=0.

Hence

(10.110)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big(\mathcal{L}[Z^{[N],1}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))]-\mathcal{L}[Z^{[N],2}(\bar{L}(N_{k}))]\Big)=\delta_{0}

and therefore

(10.111)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\bigl(Z^{[N_{k}]}(N_{k}s)\bigr)=\int_{[0,1]}P_{{\bf\Theta^{\mathrm{eff}}}(s)}({\rm d}\rho)\int\nu_{\rho,{\bf y_{1}}}P_{Y_{1}(s)}^{\rho}({\rm d}{\bf y_{1}}).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
10.6
.
∎

Like for the one-colour mean-field system, Proposition
10.6
and Lemmas
10.7
–
10.13
give rise to the following corollary, which will be important to derive the evolution of the
1
-blocks on time scale
Ns
.

Corollary 10.14
.

Fix
s>0
. Let
\mu_{N}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of

(10.112)

Z^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))=(X^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),Y_{0}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N)),Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns-L(N))),

and let
\mu
be a weak limit point of the sequence
(\mu_{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let

(10.113)

\Theta=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x_{i}^{\mu}+Ky_{i}^{\mu}}{1+K}\,\qquad\text{ in }L_{2}(\mu),

and let
(Z^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}=(X^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t),Y_{0}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t),Y_{1}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}
be the infinite system evolving according to (
10.74
) starting from its equilibrium measure. Consider the finite system
Z^{[N]}
as a system on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
by periodic continuation. Construct
(Z^{[N]}(t))_{t>0}
and
(Z^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}
on one probability space. Then, for all
t\geq 0
,

(10.114)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|x_{i}^{[N]}(Ns+t)-x_{i}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t)\right|\right]+K_{0}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|y_{i,0}^{[N]}(Ns+t)-y_{i,0}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle+K_{1}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|y_{i,1}^{[N]}(Ns+t)-y_{i,1}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t)\right|\right]=0\qquad\forall\,i\in[N].

Proof.

Proceed as in the proof of Corollary
9.1
, but use the setup of the two-colour mean-field system and therefore replace Proposition
8.4
, Lemma
8.11
and Lemma
8.13
by, respectively Proposition
10.6
, Lemma
10.12
and Lemma
10.13
.
∎

Step 4: Limiting evolution of the
1
-blocks.

Lemma 10.15
.

[Limiting evolution of the 1-blocks]

Let
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s))_{s>0}
be the process defined in (
10.21
) with initial state

(10.115)

z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0)=(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}).

Then

(10.116)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s))_{s>0}\right].

Proof.

By
[
YW71
]
, the SSDE in (
10.21
) has a unique strong solution. Therefore the process
(z^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s))_{s>0}
is Markov. Its generator
G
is given by

(10.117)

G=\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\,(y-x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x}+e_{1}(x-y)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial y}+\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\,(\mathcal{F}g)(x)\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}},

and hence
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s))_{s\geq 0}
solves the martingale problem for
G
. We will use
[
JM86
, Theorem 3.3.1]
, to prove that (
10.116
) holds.

Define

(10.118)

(\vartheta_{0}^{N},\vartheta_{y_{1}}^{N})=\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(0),\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(0)\right).

Since we start from an i.i.d. law, by the law of large numbers we have that

(10.119)

\lim_{N\to\infty}{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(0)=\lim_{N\to\infty}(\vartheta_{0}^{N},\vartheta_{y_{1}}^{N})=\left(\vartheta_{0},\theta_{y_{1}}\right)\qquad a.s.

By the SSDE in (
10.37
) and an optional sampling argument, we have, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(10.120)

\lim_{s\downarrow 0}\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns),\Theta^{(1),[N]}(Ns)\right)=(\vartheta_{0}^{N},\vartheta_{y_{1}}^{N})\quad\text{ a.s.}

Therefore we can continuously extend the process
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns))_{s>0}
to
0
and, in particular,

(10.121)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(0)\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0)\right].

Since we already showed that the processes

(10.122)

\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)_{s>0}

are
\mathcal{D}
-semimartingales, and are trivially bounded, we are left to show that

(10.123)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{0}^{s}{\rm d}r\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}\big(f,{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr),r,\cdot\big)-(Gf)\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Nr)\big)\Big|\Big]=0.

Here,
G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}
is the operator defined in (
10.39
). Since we are working on the space
\mathcal{C}^{*}
of polynomials on
[0,1]^{2}
, all derivatives of
f\in\mathcal{C}^{*}
are bounded. Hence, by dominated convergence, it is enough to prove that, for all
s>0
,

(10.124)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{[N]}\Big[\Big|G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}\big(f,{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns),s,\cdot\big)-(Gf)\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\Big|\Big]=0.

Note that

(10.125)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}\big(f,{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns),s,\cdot\big)-(Gf)\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg|\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\,\Big[\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x_{i}(Ns,\omega)\Big]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)

\displaystyle+e_{1}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x_{i}(Ns,\omega)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)\Big]\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x_{i}(Ns,\omega))\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\big[\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)\big]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)

\displaystyle-e_{1}\big[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{1}}(Ns)\big]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}(\mathcal{F}g)(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns))\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\Bigg|\Bigg].

Hence

(10.126)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big|G^{(1),[N]}_{\dagger}\big(f,{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns),s,\cdot\big)-(Gf)\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\Big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\,\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x_{i}(Ns,\omega)\right|\,\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle+\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[e_{1}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x_{i}(Ns,\omega)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)\right|\,\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle+\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x_{i}(Ns,\omega))-(\mathcal{F}g)\big(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\right|\,\left|\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}\big({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\big)\right|\right].

Note that each of the derivatives is bounded by aconstant because we work on
\mathcal{C}^{*}
. The first and the second term tend to zero by Lemma
10.9
. For the third term we can use a similar argument as used in (
9.97
), since we showed Lemmas
10.7
–
10.13
for the single components in the mean-field system with two colours.
∎

Step 5: Evolution of the averages in the Meyer-Zheng topology.

In this section we prove the following proposition

Proposition 10.16
.

[Convergence in the Meyer-Zheng topology]

Suppose that the effective estimator process defined in (
10.15
) satisfies

(10.127)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N]}(Ns)\right)_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right].

Then for the averages in (
10.7
),

(10.128)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}^{[N]}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{1}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right]

\displaystyle\text{in the Meyer-Zheng topology},

where the limiting process
\left(z_{1}(s)\right)_{s>0}
is defined as in (
10.20
).

To prove Proposition
10.16
we need the following characterisation of continuous functions in the Meyer-Zheng topology

Lemma 10.17
.

[Convergence of marginals in the Meyer-Zheng topology]

Let
(E,d)
be a Polish space with metric
d
. Suppose that
(X_{n}(s),Y_{n}(s))_{s>0}
is a stochastic process with state space
E^{2}
. If

(10.129)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[(X_{n}(s),Y_{n}(s))_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(X(s),Y(s))_{s>0}\right]\text{ in the Meyer-Zheng topology},

then the marginals also converge in the Meyer-Zheng topology, i.e.,

(10.130)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[(X_{n}(s))_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(X(s))_{s>0}\right]\text{ in the Meyer-Zheng topology},

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[(Y_{n}(s))_{s>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(Y(s))_{s>0}\right]\text{ in the Meyer-Zheng topology}.

The proof of Lemma
10.17
is given in Appendix
B.3
.

Proof of Proposition
10.16
.

By Lemma
10.9
, we have that, for all
s>0
,

(10.131)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns)-x_{1}^{[N]}(s)\right|\right]=0

and

(10.132)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns)-y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s)\right|\right]=0.

Applying Lemmas
8.19
,
8.20
and
8.21
, like in the proof of Proposition
8.18
, we obtain

(10.133)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(x_{1}^{[N]}(s),y_{0,1}^{[N]}(s),\bar{\Theta}^{[N]}(Ns),\Theta^{[N]}_{y_{1,1}}(Ns)\right)_{s>0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s),x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s),x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(s),y_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(s)\right)_{s>0}\right]\text{ in the Meyer-Zheng topology.}

Applying Lemma
10.17
, we get the claim.
∎

Step 6: Proof of the two-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
10.2
(a) follows directly from Lemma
10.15
. The proof of Proposition
10.2
(b) is a consequence of Proposition
10.2
(d). The proof of Prosition
10.2
(c) follows from Proposition
10.2
(a) by applying Proposition
10.16
. The proof of Proposition (
10.2
)(d) follows by the same argument as used in the proof Proposition
8.1
(c) in Section
9.4
. In this argument we have to replace the two-component system
Z^{[N]}(Ns+t)=(X^{[N]}(Ns+t),Y^{[N]}(Ns+t))_{t\geq 0}
by the three-component system
Z^{[N]}(Ns+t)=(X^{[N]}(Ns+t),Y_{0}^{[N]}(Ns+t),Y_{1}^{[N]}(Ns+t))_{t\geq 0}
and use the infinite system defined in
10.11
instead of the infinite system defined in (
8.1
). We now use the two-dimensional transition kernel in (
10.30
), which controls the transition probabilities of the two-dimensional process
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1)}(s),\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1)}(s))_{s>0}
, instead of the one-dimensional transition kernel in (
8.18
).
∎

11
Preparation:
N\to\infty
, two-level three-colour

To get a proper understanding of how the migration comes into play on different space-time scales, we next look at a two-level mean-field system where the geographic space consists of two layers and the seed-bank consist of three layers, corresponding three colours
0
,
1
,
2
. In Section
11.1
we give the set-up of the two-level three-colour mean-field model.
In Section
11.2
we give a scheme to prove the analysis of the two-level three-colour mean-field model. Finally, in Section
11.3
we prove the steps of the scheme given in Section
11.2
.

11.1
Two-level three-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme

We consider a restricted version of the SSDE in (
2.20
) on the finite geographic space

(11.1)

[N^{2}]=\{0,1,\ldots,N^{2}-1\},\qquad N\in\mathbb{N}.

This space should be interpreted as grouping the
N
-blocks consisting of
N
colonies together, i.e.

(11.2)

[N^{2}]=\bigcup_{l=0}^{N-1}\{Nl,Nl+1,\cdots,Nl+N-1\}.

With this interpretation we can use the metric
d_{[N^{2}]}
that is induced by the metric
d_{\Omega_{N}}
on the hierarchical group
\Omega_{N}
(recall (
2.4
)). The migration kernel
a^{\Omega_{N}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is restricted to
[N^{2}]
by setting all migration rates outside the 2-block equal to
0
, i.e.,
c_{k}=0
for all
k\geq 2
. Hence the migration kernel is given by

(11.3)

a^{[N^{2}]}(i,j)=1_{\{d_{[N^{2}]}(i,j)\leq 1\}}\frac{c_{0}}{N}+\frac{c_{1}}{N^{3}},

where
c_{0},c_{1}\in(0,\infty)
are constants. The seed-bank of the restricted system consists of
three colours
, labeled
0

1
and
2
, with exchange rates given by
K_{0}e_{0},e_{0}
,
\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N},\frac{e_{1}}{N}
and
\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}},\frac{e_{2}}{N^{2}}
respectively. The state space of the restricted system is

(11.4)

S=\mathfrak{s}^{[N^{2}]},\qquad\mathfrak{s}=[0,1]\times[0,1]^{3},

and the restricted system is denoted by

(11.5)

\displaystyle(Z^{[N^{2}]}(t))_{t\geq 0}

\displaystyle=\Big(X^{[N^{2}]}(t),\left(Y_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(t),Y_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t),Y_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right)\Big)_{t\geq 0},

\displaystyle\Big(X^{[N^{2}]}(t),\left(Y_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(t),Y_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t),Y_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t),\left(y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t),y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t),y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)\right)\Big)_{i\in[N^{2}]}.

The components of the restricted system
(Z^{[N^{2}]}(t))_{t\geq 0}
evolve according to the SSDE

(11.6)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)=\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}1_{\{d_{[N^{2}]}(i,j)\leq 1\}}[x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{1}}{N^{3}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}[x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)=\frac{e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N^{2}],

which is a special case of (
2.20
). By
[
SS80
, Theorem 3.1]
, the SSDE in (
11.6
) is the unique solution. It is important to note that we can write the SSDE also

(11.7)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)=c_{0}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in[N]_{i}}x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{1}}{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)=\frac{e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N^{2}],

where
[N]_{i}
denotes the set of colonies in the 1-block around site
i\in[N^{2}]
. Therefore the migration term for a single colony in the two-level mean-field system can be interpreted as a drift towards the
1
-block average of the active population at rate
c_{0}
and a drift towards the
2
-block average of the active population at rate
\frac{c_{1}}{N}
. We are interested in (
11.7
) on time scales
N^{0}
,
N
and
N^{2}
. On time scale
N^{0}
we will look at the single colonies, i.e., space-time scale
0
. On time scale
N
we will look at the
1
-block averages, i.e., space-time scale
1
and on time scale
N^{2}
we will look at the
2
-block averages, i.e., space-time scale
2
. In the sequel we will focus on site
0
, the 1-block around site
0
and the 2-block around site
0
. We will suppress this site from the notation, but instead use subscripts
0
,
1
,
2
to indicate when we look at a single colony, a 1-block average or a 2-block average. We will use the convention that in the subscript of a dormant population the first subscript denotes the colour and the second subscript denotes the level of the block, so
y_{0,1}
is the
1
-block average around site
0
of the dormant population with colour
0
, while
y_{1,0}
is the
1
-dormant single colony at site
0
. Heuristically, we can read off the following results from the SSDE in (
11.7
).

\bullet

On time scale
1=N^{0}
(i.e., space-time scale
0
) in the limit as
N\to\infty
, the colour-
1
dormant population and the colour-
2
dormant population do not yet move. Hence

(11.8)

\big(y^{[N^{2}]}_{1,0}(t_{0}),y^{[N^{2}]}_{2,0}(t_{0})\big)_{t_{0}\geq 0},

converges as
N\to\infty
to the constant processes on time scale
t_{0}
. Therefore the colour
1
-dormant population and the colour
2
-dormant population are both slow seed-banks on space-time scale
0
. The components
((x_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{0}),y_{0,0}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{0})))_{t_{0}\geq 0}
converge to i.i.d. copies of the single-colony McKean-Vlasov process in (
7.1
), where in the corresponding SSDE the parameters
e,K,c
are replaced by
c_{0},e_{0},K_{0}
and
E=1
. So, on time scale
1
we only see the colour
0
-dormant population evolve. Therefore the colour-
0
dormant population is the
effective seed-bank
on time scale
t_{0}
. The process

(11.9)

(z^{[N^{2}]}_{0}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}=(x^{[N^{2}]}_{0}(t_{0}),y^{[N^{2}]}_{0,0}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0},

will be called
the single colony effective process.

\bullet

On time scale
N^{1}
(i.e., space-time scale
1
), we look at the averages

(11.10)

\displaystyle(z_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}=\left(x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1}),\left(y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1}),y_{1,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1}),y_{2,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right)\right)_{t_{1}>0}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}),\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(Nt_{1}),\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(Nt_{1}),\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(Nt_{1})\right)\right)_{t_{1}>0}.

(Recall Remark
2.4
to appreciate the notation.) We use the lower index
1
to indicate that the average is the analogue of the 1-block average defined in (
2.22
). Using (
11.6
), we see that the dynamics of the system in (
11.10
) is given by the SSDE

(11.11)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=c_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}x_{j}(Nt_{1})-x_{1}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x_{i}(Nt_{1}))}\,{\rm d}w(t_{1})

\displaystyle+NK_{0}e_{0}\left[y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\left[y_{1,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N}\left[y_{2,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=Ne_{0}\left[x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-y_{1,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{2,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{e_{2}}{N}\left[x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})-y_{1,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\right]{\rm d}t_{1}.

In the limit
N\to\infty
we expect that the colour
2
-dormant population does not move, since it only interacts with the active population at rate
\frac{e_{2}}{N}
. Therefore we expect
(y_{2,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
to converge to a constant process and hence we say that the colour
2
-dormant population behaves like a
slow seed-bank
. The colour
1
-dormant population, however, has a non-trivial interaction with the active population and therefore is the
effective seed-bank
on space-time scale
1
. The colour
0
-dormant population has, in the limit as
N\to\infty
, an infinitely strong interaction with the active population. Therefore we expect that, in the limit as
N\to\infty
, its path becomes rougher and rougher at rarer and rarer times. We will need to use the
Meyer-Zheng topology
to prove that

(11.12)

\lim_{N\to\infty}y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})=\lim_{N\to\infty}x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})\text{ for most }t_{1}.

Therefore the colour
0
-dormant population equalizes with the active population, due to its infinitely strong interaction with the active population. Hence at space-time scale
1
, the colour
0
-dormant population behaves like a
fast seed-bank.
If we look at the active population, then we see that it feels a drift towards the
2
-block average of the active population, and resamples at a rate that is the 1-block average of the resampling rates in the single colonies. Furthermore, in the limit as
N\to\infty
, it feels an infinitely fast drift towards the colour
0
-dormant population, has a non-trivial interaction with the colour
1
-dormant population, and its interaction with the colour
2
-dormant population cancels out. As long as we focus on the combination

\frac{x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})+K_{0}y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})}{1+K_{0}},

we see that the colour-
0
terms with the factor
N
in front cancel out. This will allow us to do most of the analysis in the path space topology, without using the Meyer-Zheng topology. The process

\left(\frac{x_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})+K_{0}y_{0,1}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{1})}{1+K_{0}},y^{[N^{2}]}_{1,1}(t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0}

will therefore be called the
effective process
.

\bullet

On time scale
N^{2}
(i.e., space-time scale
2
) we look at the equivalent of the
2
-block averages in (
2.22
),

(11.13)

\displaystyle\left(x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2}),(y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2}),y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2}),y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2}))\right)_{t_{2}>0}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}t_{2}),

\displaystyle\Bigg(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(N^{2}t_{2}),\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(N^{2}t_{2}),\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(N^{2}t_{2})\Bigg)\Bigg)_{t_{2}>0},

which evolves according to the SSDE

(11.14)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x_{i}(N^{2}t_{2}))}\,{\rm d}w(t_{2})

\displaystyle+N^{2}K_{0}e_{0}\left[y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2}

\displaystyle+NK_{1}e_{1}\left[y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}e_{2}\left[y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})

\displaystyle=N^{2}e_{0}\left[x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})

\displaystyle=Ne_{1}\left[x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2},

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})

\displaystyle=e_{2}\left[x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2}.

In this case we see that migration in the active component cancels out and the resampling rate is given by the average over the complete population. In the limit as
N\to\infty
, we see that the active population interacts at an infinitely fast rate with the
0
-dormant population as well as with the colour
1
-dormant population. Hence both the colour
0
and the colour
1
seed-banks are fast seed-banks and we expect equalisation of the active population and the colour
0
-dormant population and the colour
1
-dormant population in Meyer-Zheng topology. The active population, in the limit as
N\to\infty
, has a non-trivial interaction with the colour
2
-dormant population, and hence the colour
2
-dormant population is the
effective seed-bank
on time scale
N^{2}
. Looking at the quantity

(11.15)

\frac{x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{0}y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{1}y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}},

for which we find

(11.16)
