\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}\left[\frac{x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{0}y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{1}y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x_{i}(N^{2}t_{2}))}\,{\rm d}w(t_{2})+K_{2}e_{2}\left[y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})-x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right]{\rm d}t_{2},

we see that the infinite rates cancel out. We will call

(11.17)

\left(\frac{x_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{0}y_{0,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})+K_{1}y_{1,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}},y_{2,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}

the effective process.
Using the effective process we can analyse our system in path space.

\blacktriangleright
Scaling limit.

Let
(z_{0}(t))_{t\geq 0}=(x_{0}(t),(y_{0,0}(t),y_{1,0}(t),y_{2,0}(t)))_{t\geq 0}
be the process evolving according to

(11.18)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x_{0}(t)=c_{0}\,[\theta-x_{0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x_{0}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)

\displaystyle+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y_{0,0}(t)-x_{0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y_{0,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x_{0}(t)-y_{0,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y_{1,0}(t)=y_{1,0},

\displaystyle y_{2,0}(t)=y_{2,0},

where
\theta\in[0,1]
,
y_{1,0}\in[0,1]
and
y_{2,0}\in[0,1]
. The process
(z_{0}(t))_{t\geq 0})
will be the limiting process for the single colonies. The corresponding single colony effective processes are given by

(11.19)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=c_{0}[\theta-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0,0}(t)-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0,0}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{0}\,[x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0}(t)-y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{0,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

where
\theta\in[0,1]
. By
[
YW71
]
, (
11.18
) and (
11.19
) have a unique strong solution. Like for the one-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme, we need the following list of ingredients to formally state the multi-scale analysis:

1.

For positive times
t>0
, we define the following
1
-block estimators
for the finite system:

(11.20)

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)+K_{0}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)}{1+K_{0}},

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{x}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t).

We abbreviate

(11.21)

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left({\Theta}_{x}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t),\left(\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)\right)\right),

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)\right),

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t)\right).

We call
({\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
the
1
-block estimator process
,
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
the
auxiliary
1
-block estimator process
and
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
the
effective
1
-block estimator process
. The auxiliary
1
-block estimator will be useful in the proofs. For
t>0
, we define the following
2
-block estimators
for the finite system:

(11.22)

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\frac{x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)+K_{0}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)+K_{1}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}},

\displaystyle\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t).

We abbreviate

(11.23)

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left({\Theta}_{x}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t),\left(\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(t),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)\right)\right),

\displaystyle{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(t)\right).

We call
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
the
effective
2
-block estimator process
and
({\bf\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
as the
2
-block estimator process
.

2.

The
time scale

N
for which
\mathcal{L}[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Nt_{1}-L(N))-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Nt_{1})]=\delta_{0}
for all
L(N)
such that
L(N)\to\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, but not for
L(N)=N
. In words,
N
is the time scale on which
\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(\cdot)
starts evolving, i.e.,
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
is no longer a fixed process. When we use time scale
N
, we will use
t_{1}
as a time index, which indicates the “faster time scale”. For the “slow time scale” we use
t_{0}
as time index.

The
time scale

N^{2}
for which
\mathcal{L}[\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-L(N)N)-\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})]=\delta_{0}
for all
L(N)
such that
L(N)\to\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, but not for
L(N)=N
. In words,
N^{2}
is the time scale on which
\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(\cdot)
starts evolving, i.e.,
(\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0},
is no longer a fixed process. When we use time scale
N^{2}
, we will use
t_{2}
as a time index, which indicates the “fastest time scale”.

3.

The
invariant measure
for the evolution of a single colony in (
11.18
), written

(11.24)

\Gamma_{\theta,{\bf y_{0}}}^{(0)},\qquad{\bf y_{0}}=(\theta,y_{1,0},y_{2,0}),

and the invariant measure of the level-
0
effective process evolving according to (
11.19
), written

(11.25)

\Gamma_{\theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(0)}.

4.

The renormalisation transformation
\mathcal{F}\colon\,\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}
,

(11.26)

(\mathcal{F}g)(\theta)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}g(x)\,\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff},(0)}_{\theta}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}y_{0}),\quad\theta\in[0,1],

where
\Gamma_{\theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(0)}
is the equilibrium measure in (
11.25
). Note that this is the same transformation as defined in (
4.16
), but defined for the truncated system. Later we will study iterates of the renormalisation transformation. Therefore we will write
\mathcal{F}^{(1)}g=\mathcal{F}g
, to indicate that we apply the renormalisation transformation only once.

5.

The limiting
1
-block process is given by
(z_{1}(t))_{t>0})=(x_{1}(t),(y_{0,1}(t),y_{1,1}(t),y_{2,1}(t)))_{t>0}
and evolves according to

(11.27)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg[c_{1}[\theta-x_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}^{(1)}g)(x_{1}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\,[y_{1,1}(t)-x_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t\Bigg],

\displaystyle y_{0,1}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{1}(t),

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,1}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\,[x_{1}(t)-y_{1,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y_{2,1}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{2,1},

where
\theta\in[0,1]
, and
y_{2,1}\in[0,1]
, and
\mathcal{F}^{(1)}
is the renormalisation transformation defined in (

). The limiting
1
-block process for the auxiliary estimator process is given by
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{t>0}=(x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t),y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1}(t),y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{2,1}(t))_{t>0}
and evolves according to

(11.28)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg[c_{1}[\theta-x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}^{(1)}g)(x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\,[y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1}(t)-x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\,[x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t)-y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{2,1}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{2,1},

for
\theta\in[0,1]
. The auxiliary estimator process turns out to be important in the next section. The effective limiting
1
-block process is given by
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t))_{t>0})=(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t),y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(t))_{t>0}
and evolves according to

(11.29)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg[c_{1}[\theta-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}^{(1)}g)(x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\,[y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(t)-x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t\Bigg],

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\,[x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1}(t)-y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{1,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

for
\theta\in[0,1]
. By
[
YW71
]
, (
11.27
), (
11.28
) and (
11.29
) have a unique strong solution.

6.

The
invariant measure
of the infinite system in (

), written

(11.30)

\Gamma_{\theta,y_{1}}^{(1)},\qquad y_{1}=(\theta,\theta,y_{2,1}),

and the invariant measures of the level-

) and the level-

),

(11.31)

??,\,\Gamma_{\theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1)}.

7.

The first iteration of the renormalisation transformation,

(11.32)

(\mathcal{F}^{(2)}g)(\theta)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(\mathcal{F}g)(x)\,\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff},(1)}_{\theta}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}y_{1}),\quad\theta\in[0,1].

Hence

(11.33)

(\mathcal{F}^{(2)}g)(\theta)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff},(1)}_{\theta}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v)\int_{[0,1]^{2}}g(x)\,\Gamma^{\mathrm{eff},(0)}_{u}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}y).

The limiting
2
-block process
(z_{2}(t))_{t>0}=(x_{2}(t),(y_{0,2}(t),y_{1,2}(t),y_{2,2}(t)))_{t>0}

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\left[\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}^{(2)}g)\big(x_{2}(t)\big)}\,{\rm d}w(t)+K_{2}e_{2}\left[y_{2,2}(t)-x_{2}(t)\right]{\rm d}t\right],

\displaystyle y_{0,2}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{2}(t),

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{1,2}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{2}(t),

\displaystyle{\rm d}y_{2,2}(t)

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{2,2}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{2}\,[x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{2}(t)-y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{2,2}(t)]\,{\rm d}t.

We are now ready to state the scaling limit for the evolution of the averages in (

).

Proposition 11.1
.

[Mean-field: two-level three-colour finite-systems scheme]

Suppose that
\mu(0)=\mu^{\otimes[N^{2}]}
for some
\mu\in\mathcal{P}\left([0,1]\times[0,1]^{2}\right)
. Let

(11.36)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{0}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[\frac{x+K_{0}y_{0}}{1+K_{0}}\right],\quad\vartheta_{1}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[\frac{x+K_{0}y_{0}+K_{1}y_{1}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\right],

\displaystyle\theta_{y_{1}}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[y_{1}\right],\quad\theta_{y_{2}}=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}\left[y_{2}\right].

and recall the limiting process
(z_{2}(t))_{t>0}
in (
11.34
) and the limiting process
(z_{1}(t))_{t>0}
in (
11.27
).
Assume for the
2
-dormant
1
-blocks that

(11.37)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[Y_{2,1}^{[N^{2}]}(Nt_{2})\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right]=P^{z_{2}(t_{2})},

and for the
2
-dormant
0
-blocks (= single colonies) that

(11.38)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[Y_{2,0}^{[N^{2}]}(Nt_{2}+Nt_{1})\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right]=P^{z_{1}(t_{1})}.

Then the following hold:

(a)

For the effective
2
-block estimator process defined in (
11.23
),

(11.39)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{2}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right],

where the limit is determined by the unique solution of the SSDE (
11.35
) with initial state

(11.40)

\displaystyle z_{2}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0)=\left(x_{2}^{\mathrm{eff}}(0),y^{\mathrm{eff}}_{2}(0)\right)=\left(\vartheta_{1},\theta_{y_{2}}\right).

(b)

For the effective
1
-block estimator process defined in (
11.21
),

(11.41)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0}\,\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}\right],

where, conditional on
x^{\mathrm{eff}}_{2}(t_{2})=u
, the limit process is the unique solution of the SSDE in (
11.29
) with
\theta
replaced by
u
and with initial measure
\Gamma_{u}^{\mathrm{eff},(1)}
.

(c)

For the single colony effective process defined in (

),

(11.42)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{0}^{\mathrm{eff},[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}+t_{0})\right)_{t_{0}\geq 0}\,\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z_{0}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}\right],

where, conditional on
x_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{1})=v
, the limit process is the unique solution of the SSDE in (
11.19
) with
\theta
replaced by
v
and with initial measure
\Gamma_{v}^{\mathrm{eff},(0)}
.

(d)

For the
2
-block estimator process defined in (
11.23
),

(11.43)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[\left(z_{2}(t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right]

\displaystyle\text{in the Meyer-Zheng topology},

where the limit process is the unique solution of the SSDE in (
11.34
) with initial state

(11.44)

z_{2}(0)=\left(\vartheta_{1},(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{1},\theta_{y_{2}})\right).

(e)

Fix
t_{2}>0
. Assume (
11.37
). Define

(11.45)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})=

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{4}}S^{[2]}_{t_{2}}\bigl((\vartheta_{1},(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{1},\theta_{y_{2}})),{\rm d}(u_{x},u_{x},u_{x},u_{y_{2,2}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]}P^{(u_{x},u_{x},u_{x},u_{y_{2,2}})}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,\Gamma^{(1)}_{(u_{x},(u_{x},u_{x},y_{2,1}))}\in\mathcal{P}([0,1]^{4}),

where
\Gamma^{(1)}_{(u_{x},(u_{x},u_{x},y_{2,1}))}

11.30
) and
S^{[2]}_{t_{2}}((\vartheta_{1},(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{1},\theta_{y_{2}})),\cdot)

t_{2}
law of the limiting process
(z_{2}(t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}
in (
11.43
) starting from
(\vartheta_{1},(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{1},\theta_{y_{2}}))\in[0,1]\times[0,1]^{3}
.

Let
(z^{\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}\geq 0}
be the random process that conditioned on
z_{2}(t_{2})=(\theta,(\theta,\theta,y_{2,2}))
moves according to (

) with
\theta=\theta
and
y_{2,1}(0)=y_{2,1}

(which is a mixture of random processes in equilibrium). Then for the
1
-block estimator process defined in (

),

(11.46)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z^{\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}\right]

\displaystyle\text{ in the Meyer-Zheng topology}.

(f)

Let
z_{1}(t_{1})
be the limiting process obtained in (e). Assume (
11.38
). Define, for

,

(11.47)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(0)}(t_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{4}}\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})({\rm d}z_{1})\,\int_{[0,1]}P^{z_{1}}({\rm d}y_{2,0})\,\Gamma^{(0)}_{(x_{1},(x_{1},y_{1,1}.y_{2,0}))},

where
\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})
is as defined in (

). Let

which is a mixture of random processes in equilibrium. Then

(11.48)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\Big[\Big(z_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}+t_{0})\Big)_{t_{0}\geq 0}\Big]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z^{\Gamma^{(0)}(t_{2})}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}\right].

Remark 11.2
.

Note that Proposition
11.1
(f) does not depend on the choice of
t_{1}
, because
\Gamma^{(1)}(t_{2})
is already a mixture of equilibrium measures of the
1
-block process.

\blacksquare

Remark 11.3
.

Note that in Propostion
11.1
(f)
\Gamma^{(0)}_{(x_{1},(x_{1},y_{1,1},y_{2,0}))}
is the equilibrium measure of (
11.18
) (see also (
11.24
)), where
y_{1,0}=y_{1,1}
. This means that all colour
1
-dormant single colonies equal the current state of the colour
1
-dormant
1
-block. We say that given the state of the
1
-dormant
1
-block, the
1
-dormant single colonies become deterministic. This effect occurs once a slow seed-bank, in this case the colour
1
seed-bank, is already in equilbrium on the space-time scale where it is effective, in this case space-time-scale
1
. Since we start at times
N^{2}t_{2}
, the
1
-dormant
1
-blocks are already in equilibrium. This will turn out to be the reason that the single colour
1
-dormant colonies are equal to the current value of the
1
-dormant
1
-block averages. Note that at time
N^{2}t_{2}
the
2
-dormant
2
-blocks do not yet have reached equilibrium. Hence the colour
2
-dormant
1
-blocks and the colour
2
-dormant single colonies do not equal the instantaneous value of the
2
-dormant
2
-block averages. In the Section
11.3.8
we will treat this effect in detail.

\blacksquare

11.2
Scheme for the two-level three-colour mean-field analysis.

In this section we give a scheme to prove Proposition
11.1
. The proof of the steps in the scheme will be written in Section
11.3
. To analyse the two-level hierarchical mean-field system we use the results obtained in Sections
8.2
,
9
and
10.2
.

The scheme for the two-level three-colour hierarchical mean-field system comes in 11 steps. Recall the estimators defined in (
11.20
) and (
11.22
).

1.

Tightness of the effective
2
-block estimator processes

(11.49)

\left(\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=\left(\left(\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}),\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}.

2.

Stability property of the 2-block estimators, i.e., for
L(N)
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
,

(11.50)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt)\right|=0\text{ in probability}

and

(11.51)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt)\right|=0\text{ in probability. }

3.

Tightness of the effective
1
-block estimator process (recall (
11.21
)),

(11.52)

\left(\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0}\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}.

4.

Stability property of
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
, i.e., for
L(N)
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
, for all
\epsilon>0
,

(11.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0

\displaystyle\text{ in probability},

(11.54)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0

\displaystyle\text{ in probability},

(11.55)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(1),[N]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0

\displaystyle\text{ in probability}.

5.

Recall that there are
N
1-blocks in
[N^{2}]
. Since tightness of components implies tightness of the process, step 3 implies that the full
1
-block process

(11.56)

\left(\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,\,i\in[N]}\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight. From the tightness in steps 1 and 3 we can construct a subsequence
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
along which

(11.57)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right)_{t_{2}>0}\right],

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,\,i\in[N_{k}]}\right]

both exists. Define the measure

(11.58)

\nu^{(0)}(t_{2})=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Gamma^{(0)}_{i}(t_{2}).

Show that along the same subsequence the single components converge to the infinite system, i.e.,

(11.59)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\Big[\Big(Z^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}+t_{0})\Big)_{t_{0}\geq 0}\Big]=\mathcal{L}\left[(Z^{\nu^{(0)}(t_{2})}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}\right].

Here,
(Z^{\nu^{(0)}(t_{2})}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}
is the process starting from
\nu^{(0)}(t_{2})
with components evolving according to (
11.18
), where
\theta
is now a random variable that inherits its law from

(11.60)

{}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}))_{i\in[N_{k}^{2}]}],

and, similarly, the laws of
y_{1,0}
and
y_{2,0}
in the limiting process
(Z^{\nu^{(0)}(t_{2})}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}
are determined by

(11.61)

{}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}))_{i\in[N_{k}^{2}]}].

6.

Use the limiting evolution of the single colonies obtained in step 5 to identify the limiting
1
-block process along the same subsequence, i.e., identify the limit

(11.62)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,i\in[N_{k}]}\right].

7.

Identify the limit
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}]
with the help of the limiting evolution of the single colonies obtained in step 5 and the limiting evolution of the full
1
-block process obtained in step 6.

8.

Prove that the
1
-dormant single colonies at time
N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}
equal, in the limit as
N\to\infty
, the
1
-dormant
1
-block averages. The proof of this step shows how the evolution of the slow seed-banks must be analysed.

9.

Show that the convergence in step 8, step 7 and step 5 actually holds along each subsequence. Therefore we obtain the limiting evolution of the single colonies, the auxiliary
1
-block process and the effective
2
-block process.

10.

Use the Meyer-Zheng topology to describe the limiting evolution of

(11.63)

\displaystyle\Big(\Theta_{x}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}),

\displaystyle\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\Big)_{t_{1}>0}

and

(11.64)

\Big(\Theta_{x}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N^{2}t_{2}),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(N^{2}t_{2}),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(N^{2}t_{2})\Big)_{t_{2}>0}.

11.

Combine the above steps to complete the proof of Proposition
11.1
.

11.3
Proof of two-level three-colour mean-field finite-systems scheme

In this section we prove the steps in the scheme given in Section
11.2
.

11.3.1
Tightness of the
2
-block estimators

In this section we prove step 1 of the scheme.

Lemma 11.4
.

[Tightness of the 2-block estimator]

Let

(11.65)

{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})=(\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}),\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(N^{2}t_{2}))

be defined as in (
11.22
). Then
(\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}])_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a tight sequence of probability measures on
\mathcal{C}((0,\infty),[0,1]^{2})
.

Proof.

To prove the tightness of the
1
-blocks, we use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
.
From (
11.6
) we find that
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
evolves according to

(11.66)

\displaystyle{\rm d}\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\sqrt{g(x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y_{i,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t)-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{e_{2}}{N^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(t)-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y_{i,2}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t.

Therefore the process
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}
evolves according to

(11.67)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t_{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}K_{2}e_{2}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y_{i,2}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{2},

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(N^{2}t_{2})=e_{2}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y_{i,2}^{[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{2}.

To use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
, we define
\mathcal{C}^{*}
as the set of polynomials on
([0,1]^{2})
. Since
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}
is a semi-martingale, by applying Itô’s formula we obtain that

({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))_{t_{2}>0}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-semi-martingale with corresponding operator

(11.68)

\displaystyle G_{\dagger}^{(2),[N^{2}]}

\displaystyle\colon\,(\mathcal{C}^{*},[0,1]^{2},(0,\infty),\Omega)\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle G_{\dagger}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(f,(x,y),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\left[y-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}t,\omega)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}

\displaystyle+e_{2}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}t,\omega)-y\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2(1+K_{0}+K_{1})^{2}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}t,\omega))\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}.

The conditions
H_{1},\ H_{2},\ H_{3}
in
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
are satisfied.
Hence tightness follows from
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
. ∎

11.3.2
Stability of the
2
-block estimators

Lemma 11.5
.

[Stability property of the
2
-block estimator]

Let
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
be defined as in (
11.23
). For any
L(N)
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
,

(11.69)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt)\right|=0\text{ in probability}

and

(11.70)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt)\right|=0\text{ in probability. }

Proof.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. From the SSDE in (
11.66
) we obtain that, for
N
large enough,

(11.71)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})-\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt)\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\Bigg|\int_{N^{2}t_{2}-Nt}^{N^{2}t_{2}}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}

\displaystyle+\int_{N^{2}t_{2}-Nt}^{N^{2}t_{2}}{\rm d}r\,\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(r)-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r)\right]\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\,\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\Bigg|\int_{N^{2}t_{2}-Nt}^{N^{2}t_{2}}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|

\displaystyle>\epsilon-\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\frac{L(N)N}{N^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\Bigg|\int_{N^{2}t_{2}-Nt}^{N^{2}t_{2}}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Bigg].

By a similar optional stopping time argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.15
, the above computation shows that (
11.69
) holds. Equation (
11.70
) holds by a similar argument as given in the proof of Lemma
10.5
. ∎

11.3.3
Tightness of the 1-block estimators

Lemma 11.6
.

[Tightness of the 1-block estimator]

Let

(11.72)

\displaystyle\bf{\displaystyle\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}),\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))

be defined as in (
11.20
). Then
(\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}])_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a tight sequence of probability measures on
\mathcal{C}((0,\infty),[0,1]^{3})
.

Proof.

To prove the tightness of the
1
-blocks, we again use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
. From (
11.11
) we find that the effective process
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}

evolves according to

(11.73)

\displaystyle{\rm d}\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}c_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(Nt_{1})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t_{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\left[\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Nt_{1})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0})}\left[\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{e_{2}}{N}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}.

To use
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
, we define
\mathcal{C}^{*}
as the set of polynomials on
([0,1]^{2})
. Since
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
is a semi-martingale, by applying Itô’s formula we obtain that
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-semi-martingale with corresponding operator

(11.74)

\displaystyle G_{\dagger}^{(1),[N^{2}]}

\displaystyle\colon\,(\mathcal{C}^{*},[0,1]^{3},(0,\infty),\Omega)\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle G_{\dagger}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(f,(x,y_{1},y_{2}),t,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(Nt,\omega)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt,\omega)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\left[y_{1}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt,\omega)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0})}\left[y_{2}(Nt,\omega)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}

\displaystyle+e_{1}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt,\omega)-y_{1}\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y_{1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{e_{2}}{N}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt,\omega)-y_{2}(Nt,\omega)\right]\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial y_{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2(1+K_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt,\omega))\,\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{2}}.

The conditions
H_{1},\ H_{2},\ H_{3}
in
[
JM86
, Proposition 3.2.3]
are satisfied as before. Hence we conclude that the sequence
(\mathcal{L}[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}])_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight.
∎

11.3.4
Stability of the
1
-block estimators

Lemma 11.7
.

[Stability property of the
1
-block estimator]

Let
{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(t)
be defined as in (
10.14
). For any
L(N)
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
,

(11.75)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0\text{ in probability},

(11.76)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\Theta_{y_{1}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0\text{ in probability},

(11.77)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1})-\Theta_{y_{2}}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}-t)\right|=0\text{ in probability}.

Proof.

Define

(11.78)

u=N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}.

From the SSDE in (
11.7
) we obtain that

(11.79)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\Bigg|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(u)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(u-t)\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\int_{u-t}^{u}{\rm d}r\,\frac{c_{1}}{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N^{2}]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{j}(r)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r)\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{u-t}^{u}{\rm d}r\,\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\left[\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(r)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(r)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r)\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{u-t}^{u}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|>\epsilon\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\,\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\int_{u-t}^{u}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|

\displaystyle>\epsilon-\frac{L(N)2(c_{1}+K_{1}e_{1}+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N})}{N(1+K_{0})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\int_{u-t}^{u}{\rm d}w_{i}(r)\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(r))}\,\Bigg|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Bigg].

Via the same optional stopping time argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.15
, the above computation shows that (
11.75
) holds. Note that the extra drift term
\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N}
does not have any influence. Equations (
11.76
)–(
11.77
) hold by a similar argument as given in the proof of Lemma
10.5
.
∎

11.3.5
Limiting evolution for the single components

Proposition 11.8
.

[Equilibrium for the infinite system]

Fix
t_{2},t_{1}>0
. Let
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
and let
L(N)
be any sequence satisfying
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
such that

(11.80)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right]=P_{t_{1},t_{2}},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(Y_{1,0}^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}),Y_{2,0}^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2})\right)\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=P^{{z}_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{1})},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N_{k})}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})-\bar{\Theta}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}-t)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|{\Theta}_{y_{1}}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})-{\Theta_{y_{1}}}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}-t)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|{\Theta}_{y_{2}}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})-{\Theta_{y_{2}}}^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}-t)\right|\Bigr]=\delta_{0},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\Big(Z^{[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}),\Big)=\nu(t_{1},t_{2}).

Then
\nu(t_{1},t_{2})
is of the form

(11.81)

\nu(t_{1},t_{2})=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}P_{t_{1},t_{2}}({\rm d}\theta^{(1)},{\rm d}\theta^{(1)}_{y})\,\int_{[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}}P^{(\theta^{(1)},\theta^{(1)}_{y})}({\rm d}{\bf y})\,\nu_{\theta,{\bf y}},

where

(11.82)

\nu_{\theta,{\bf y_{0}}}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Gamma_{(\theta,{\bf y_{0,i}})}

with
\Gamma_{(\theta,{\bf y_{0,i}})}
the equilibrium measure for the
i
’th single colony defined in (
11.24
).

Note that by step 1 and step 3 we can find a subsequence
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that the first and third line in (
11.80
) hold. The second line in (
11.80
) follows from assumptions (
11.37
) and (
11.38
). To prove Proposition
11.8
we proceed as in the proof of Proposition
10.6
, but with the finite system in (
10.4
) replaced by the system in (
11.6
) and the infinite system in (
10.11
) replaced by the system in (
11.18
). Note that Lemma
10.7
holds also for the system in (
11.18
), after adding the non-interacting component
y_{2,0}
to the equilibrium. The equivalent of Lemma
10.8
will again follow from the equivalent of Lemma
10.12
. We will derive the analogue of Lemmas
10.9
and
10.10
(see Lemma’s
11.9
and
11.10
below). Lemma
10.11
can be extended with an extra colour-
2
seed-bank estimator by using the same proof. Since the infinite system for the single colonies in the two-layer three-colour mean-field system (see (
11.88
)) equals the one for the one-layer two-colour mean-field system, up to a non-interacting component, we obtain an equivalent of Lemma
10.12
. Finally, also the equivalent of Lemma
10.13
holds under an additional assumption, see Lemma
11.11
. Finally Corollary
11.12
states the equivalent of Corollary
10.14
. With the help of the lemma’s and the corollary, the proof of Proposition
11.8
follows from the same argument as used in the proof of Proposition
10.6
.

Lemma 11.9
.

[Comparison of empirical averages]

Let
(\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{x}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}
and
(\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(t_{0}))_{t_{0}\geq 0}
be defined as in (
11.20
). Then

(11.83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{x}(0)-\Theta^{(1),[N]}_{y_{0}}(0)\right)^{2}\right]}{\rm e}^{-(K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0})t}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{1}{K_{0}e_{0}+e_{0}}\left[\frac{c_{1}}{N}+\frac{||g||}{N}+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\right]}.

Proof.

The result follows by Itô-calculus on the SSDE in (
11.6
) and the same type of argument as used in the proof of Lemma
10.9
.
∎

Like for the mean-field system with one colour, we need to compare the finite system in (
11.6
) with an infinite system. To derive the analogue of Lemma
10.10
, let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
. Define
[N]_{i}
to be the
1
-block that contains site
i\in[N^{2}]
. Since we start our system in an exchangeable measure and the dynamics are exchangeable, we will only consider the single colonies in
[N]_{0}
, the
1
-block containing the site
0\in[N^{2}]
. In the rest of the prove, we will suppress the
0
from the notation i.e.,
[N]_{0}=[N]
and
\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{0}=\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}
. Define

(11.84)

u=N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}

and let
\mu_{N}
be the measure on
([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
by continuing the configuration of

(11.85)

\displaystyle\left(Z^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N))\right)

\displaystyle=\left(X^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),\left(Y_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),Y_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),Y_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N))\right)\right)

periodically to
([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, i.e., we continue the configuration of the single colonies in the first block to
([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Let

(11.86)

\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(u-L(N))+K_{0}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(u-L(N))}{1+K_{0}}.

Let

(11.87)

(Z^{\mu_{N}}(t))_{t\geq 0}=\bigl(X^{\mu_{N}}(t),\left(Y_{0}^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{1}^{\mu_{N}}(t),Y_{2}^{\mu_{N}}(t)\right)\bigr)_{t\geq 0}

be the infinite system evolving according to

(11.88)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=c_{0}\,[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}-x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{g\big(x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)\big)}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)-x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{0}\,[x^{\mu_{N}}_{i}(t)-y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,1}(0),

\displaystyle y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,2}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{\mu_{N}}_{i,2}(0),\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

starting from initial distribution
\mu_{N}
. Then the following Lemma
11.10
is the equivalent of Lemma
10.10
for the three-colour two-layer mean-field system. In particular, the infinite system considered in Lemma
11.10
is similar to the infinite system in Lemma
10.10
. The only difference is that there is one more non-interacting component added in (
11.88
).

Lemma 11.10
.

[Comparison of finite and infinite systems]

Fix
t_{1},t_{2}>0
, and let
u=N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}
. Let
L(N)
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N
. Suppose that

(11.89)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(u)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N]}(u-t)\right|=0\ \text{ in probability}.

Then, for all
t\geq 0
,

(11.90)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f\big(Z^{\mu_{N}}(t)\big)-f\big(Z^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)+t)\big)\right]\right|=0\qquad\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C}\bigl(([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mathbb{R}\bigr).

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
10.10
and couple the finite and infinite systems by their Brownian motion, exactly as was done there. The single components in the block around site
0
of the finite process
(Z^{[N^{2}]}(t))
are evolving according to

(11.91)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)=c_{0}\,\left[\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t+c_{0}\,\left[\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t)-\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}\right]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+c_{0}\,\left[\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{x}(t)-\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(t)\right]\,{\rm d}t+\frac{c_{1}}{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}x_{j}^{[N^{2}]}(t)-x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\sqrt{g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w_{i}(t)+K_{0}e_{0}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{N}\left[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)-x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(t)\right]{\rm d}t+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)-x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)=e_{0}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,0}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)=\frac{e_{1}}{N}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,1}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)=\frac{e_{2}}{N^{2}}\,[x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(t)-y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,\qquad i\in[N].

Using this SSDE we can exactly proceed as in the proof of Lemma
10.10
to obtain the result. Note that the colour-
2
seed-bank can be treated just in the same way as the colour-
1
seed-bank in the proof of Lemma
10.10
, since its rate of interaction with the active population is even slower than the rate of interaction of the colour-
1
seed-bank.
∎

Finally, we state the equivalent of Lemma
10.13
for the three-colour two-layer mean-field system.

Lemma 11.11
.

[Coupling of finite systems]

Let

(11.92)

Z^{[N^{2}],1}=(X^{[N^{2}],1},Y_{0}^{[N^{2}],1},Y_{1}^{[N^{2}],1},Y_{2}^{[N^{2}],1})

be the finite system evolving according to (
11.6
) starting from an exchangeable initial measure. Let
\mu^{[N],1}
be the measure obtain by periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N^{2}],1}(0)
in the
1
-block around
0
. Similarly, let

(11.93)

Z^{[N^{2}],2}=(X^{[N^{2}],2},Y_{0}^{[N^{2}],2},Y_{1}^{[N^{2}],2},Y_{2}^{[N^{2}],2})

be the finite system evolving according to (
11.6
) starting from an exchangeable initial measure. Let
\mu^{[N],2}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of the configuration of
Z^{[N^{2}],1}(0)
in the
1
-block around
0
. Let
\tilde{\mu}
be any weak limit point of the sequence of measures
(\mu^{[N],1}\times\mu^{[N],2})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Define the variables
\bar{\Theta}^{[N],1}
on
(([0,1]^{4},\mu^{[N],1})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
,
\bar{\Theta}^{[N],2}
on
(([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mu^{[N],2})
and
\bar{\Theta}_{1}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{2}
on
(([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\times([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},\mu)
by

(11.94)

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{[N],1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N^{2}],1}_{i}+K_{0}y^{[N^{2}],1}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},\qquad\bar{\Theta}^{[N],2}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x^{[N^{2}],2}_{i}+K_{0}y^{[N^{2}],2}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},

\displaystyle\bar{\Theta}^{1}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x^{1}_{i}+K_{0}y^{1}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},\qquad\bar{\Theta}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{x^{2}_{i}+K_{0}y^{2}_{i,0}}{1+K_{0}},

and let
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N],1}(t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N],2}(t))_{t\geq 0}
be defined as in (
10.14
) for
Z^{[N^{2}],1}
, respectively,
Z^{[N^{2}],2}
. Suppose that

(11.95)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N)}\left(\left|\bar{\Theta}^{[N],k}(0)-\bar{\Theta}^{[N],k}(t)\right|\right)=0\ \text{ in probability},\quad k\in\{1,2\},

and suppose that
\tilde{\mu}(\{\bar{\Theta}_{1}=\bar{\Theta}_{2},\,Y^{1}_{1}=Y^{2}_{1},Y^{1}_{2}=Y^{2}_{2}\})=1
. Then, for any sequence
(t(N))_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\lim_{N\to\infty}t(N)=\infty
,

(11.96)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t(N))-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t(N))|+K_{0}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t(N))|

\displaystyle+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t(N))|+K_{2}|y^{[N],1}_{i,2}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,2}(t(N))|\bigr]=0.

Proof.

Like in the proof of Lemma
10.13
, we can show with Itô calculus that the function

(11.97)

\displaystyle t\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[|x^{[N],1}_{i}(t(N))-x^{[N],2}_{i}(t(N))|+K_{0}|y^{[N],1}_{i,0}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,0}(t(N))|

\displaystyle+K_{1}|y^{[N],1}_{i,1}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,1}(t(N))|+K_{2}|y^{[N],1}_{i,2}(t(N))-y^{[N],2}_{i,2}(t(N))|\bigr]

is monotonically decreasing. Hence we can proceed as in the proof of Lemma
8.13
to show that (
11.96
) is true.
∎

From the above couplings we can derive the following corollary, which is the analogue of Corollary
10.14
for the two-level three-colour mean-field system.

Corollary 11.12
.

Fix
t_{1},t_{2}>0
and set
u=N^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}
. Let
\mu_{N}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of

(11.98)

Z^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N))=\big(X^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),Y_{0}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),Y_{1}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N)),Y_{2}^{[N^{2}]}(u-L(N))\big),

and let
\mu
be a weak limit point of the sequence
(\mu_{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let

(11.99)

\Theta=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}\frac{x_{i}^{\mu}+Ky_{i}^{\mu}}{1+K}\,\qquad\text{ in }L^{2}(\mu),

and let
(Z^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}=(X^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t),Y_{0}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t),Y_{1}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t),Y_{2}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}
be the infinite system with components evolving according to (
11.18
) with
\theta=\Theta
and
y_{i,1,0}
and
y_{i,2,0}
determined by assumption (
11.80
) and starting from its equilibrium measure. Extend the finite system
Z^{[N^{2}]}
as a system on
([0,1]^{4})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
by periodic continuation. Construct
(Z^{[N^{2}]}(t))_{t>0}
and
(Z^{\nu_{\Theta}}(t))_{t>0}
on one probability space. Then there exists a sequence
(\bar{L}(N))_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\bar{L}(N)=\infty,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\bar{L(N)}}{N}=0
and

(11.100)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}(Ns)-x_{i}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(\bar{L}(N))\right|\right]+K_{0}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|y_{i,0}^{[N^{2}]}(Ns)-y_{i,0}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(\bar{L}(N))\right|\right]

\displaystyle+K_{1}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|y_{i,1}^{[N^{2}]}(Ns)-y_{i,1}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(\bar{L}(N))\right|\right]+K_{2}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|y_{i,2}^{[N^{2}]}(Ns)-y_{i,2}^{\nu_{\Theta}}(\bar{L}(N))\right|\right]=0,\,i\in[N].

Note that Lemmas
11.10
,
11.11
and Corollary
11.12
do not only hold for sites
i
in the
1
-block around
0
, but hold for for all sites
i\in[N^{2}]
, after we replace
\Theta^{[N]_{0}}_{0}
by
\Theta^{[N]_{i}}_{i}
.

11.3.6
Limiting evolution of the
1
-block estimator process

Proposition 11.13
.

[Limiting evolution of the 1-blocks]

Fix
t_{2}>0
. Let
(L(N))_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfy
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)=\infty
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}L(N)/N=0
. Let
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a subsequence such that

(11.101)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{eff},(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right)\right]=P_{t_{2}}(\cdot),

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[y_{2,1}^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}t_{2})\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right]=P^{z_{2}(t_{2})},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left(Y_{1,0}^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}),Y_{2,0}^{[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2})\right)\Big|{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right]=P^{{z}_{1}^{\mathrm{eff}}(t_{1})},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq L(N_{k})}\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2})-\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N^{2}_{k}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-N_{k}t)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|{\Theta}_{y_{2}}^{(2),[N^{2}_{k}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})-{\Theta_{y_{2}}}^{(2),[N^{2}_{k}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}-Nt)\right|\Bigg]=\delta_{0}.

Then, for the
1
-block around
0
,

(11.102)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v),

where
\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}
is the equilibrium measure of (
11.28
) with
\theta
replaced by
u
, and

(11.103)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}\left[(z_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0})\right],

where
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
is the process evolving according to (
11.28
) with
\theta
replaced by the random variable
\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})
and with initial measure
\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v)
, and
y_{2,1}
is a random variable.

Note that by tightness of the
2
-blocks and the assumptions in Proposition
11.1
, we can always find a subsequence
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that (
11.101
) holds and also (
11.80
) holds. To prepare for the proof of Proposition
11.13
, we prove four lemmas: Lemma
11.14
shows that the limiting
1
-block system has a unique equilibrium, Lemma
11.16
implies convergence of the active
2
-block estimator and the combined
2
-block estimator, Lemma
11.17
gives a regularity property for the
2
-block estimator, and Lemma
11.18
shows the limiting evolution of the auxiliary
1
-block estimator process. Lemma
11.20
proves equation (
11.102
). After we derive these lemmas we prove Proposition
11.13
.

Lemma 11.14
.

[1-block equilibrium]

For any initial distribution
\mu\in([0,1]^{3})
, the process
(z_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
evolving according to (
11.28
) is well defined and converges to a unique equilibrium measure

(11.104)

\lim_{t_{1}\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[z^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t_{1})]=\Gamma_{\theta,y_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}.

Proof.

By
[
YW71
]
, the SSDE in (
11.28
) has a unique strong solution. By a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
10.7
, the SSDE in (
11.28
) converges to a unique equilibrium measure
\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{\theta,y_{2,1}}
.
∎

Remark 11.15
.

[Equilibrium measure]

Note that Lemma
11.14
still holds when we allow
\theta
and
y_{2,1}
to be the random variables
\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})
and
y_{2,1}
. Assuming (
11.101
), we can derive the distributions of
\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})
and
y_{2,1}
, and we can write the equilibrium as
\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v)
. In what follows we abbreviate

(11.105)

\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2}),y_{2,1,i}}=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1,i}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1,i})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v).

\blacksquare

Lemma 11.16
.

[
2
-block averages]

Define

(11.106)

\displaystyle\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})=\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Nt_{1})}{1+K_{0}}-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1}).

Then

(11.107)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(0)\right)^{2}\right]}{\rm e}^{-e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)t_{1}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\int_{0}^{t_{1}}{\rm d}s\,2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right){\rm e}^{-2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)(t_{1}-s)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Ns)-\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Ns)\right|\right]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{1}{e_{1}}\left[\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0}+K_{1})}+\frac{||g||}{2N(1+K_{0}+K_{1})}\right]}.

Proof.

For the two-level mean-field system we have the following SSDE for the
2
-block averages:

(11.108)

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}))}\,{\rm d}\tilde{w}(t_{1})

\displaystyle+NK_{0}e_{0}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=Ne_{0}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=e_{1}\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{e_{2}}{N}\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}.

Therefore

(11.109)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right)^{2}=2\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,{\rm d}\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})+{\rm d}\left<\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right>

\displaystyle=2\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}))}\,{\rm d}\tilde{w}(t_{1})

\displaystyle+2\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,\frac{K_{1}e_{1}}{(1+K_{0})}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle+2\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0})}\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1},

\displaystyle-2\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,e_{1}\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(Nt_{1})\right]\,{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}))\,{\rm d}t_{1}.

Hence

(11.110)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\left(\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Nt_{1})}{1+K_{0}}-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right)\right]\,

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0})}\,2\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Nt_{1})-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Nt_{1})\right]\right]\,

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Nt_{1}))\right],

and therefore

(11.111)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(0)\right)^{2}\right]{\rm e}^{-2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)t_{1}}+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{-2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)(t_{1}-s)}h^{[N]}(s),

where

(11.112)

\displaystyle h^{[N]}(s)=2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Ns)\,\left(\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Ns)+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(Ns)}{1+K_{0}}-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Ns)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0})}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Ns)\,\left[\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{y_{2}}(Ns)-\Theta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Ns)\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1+K_{0})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}g(x^{[N^{2}]}_{i}(Ns))\right].

Therefore

(11.113)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(Nt_{1})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(2),[N^{2}]}_{\Sigma}(0)\right)^{2}\right]}{\rm e}^{-e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)t_{1}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\int_{0}^{t_{1}}{\rm d}s\,2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right){\rm e}^{-2e_{1}\left(\frac{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\right)(t_{1}-s)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{\Theta}^{(1),[N^{2}]}(Ns)-\Theta^{(1),[N^{2}]}_{x}(Ns)\right|\right]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{1}{e_{1}}\left[\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N(1+K_{0}+K_{1})}+\frac{||g||}{2N(1+K_{0}+K_{1})}\right]}.

∎

Let
\mu_{N_{k}}
be the measure obtained by periodic continuation of the configuration

(11.114)

\displaystyle Z^{[N^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}).

Since the state space
([0,1]\times[0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is compact, we can pass to a further subsequence, to obtain

(11.115)

\mu=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mu_{N_{k}}.

Lemma 11.17
.

[Regularity for 2-block estimator]

Let
\mu
and
\mu_{N}
be as defined above. Let
(x_{i},y_{1,i},y_{2,i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
be distributed according to
\mu
. Define the random variable

(11.116)

\displaystyle\phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{1}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n^{2}]}\frac{x_{i}+K_{0}y_{i,0}+K_{1}y_{i,1}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}},\qquad\phi_{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n^{2}]}y_{i,2},

and the random variable
\phi^{[N]}
on
(\mu_{N},([0,1]^{3})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})
by putting

(11.117)

\displaystyle\phi^{[N^{2}]}=(\phi^{[N^{2}]}_{1},\phi^{[N^{2}]}_{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{1}^{[N^{2}]}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\frac{x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}+K_{0}y_{i,0}^{[N^{2}]}+K_{1}y_{i,1}^{[N^{2}]}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}},\qquad\phi_{2}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}y^{[N^{2}]}_{i,2}.

Then

(11.118)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[\phi^{[N^{2}]}]=\mathcal{L}[\phi].

Proof.

Use a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
10.11
.
∎

We will first determine the limiting evolution of
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
. To do so we consider all the
N_{k}

1
-blocks in
[N_{k}^{2}]
. After that we show that

(11.119)

{}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right)_{i\in[N_{k}]}\right]=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2}),y_{2,1,i}},

The limiting
1
-block process for the auxiliary estimator process (recall (
11.28
)) is given by

(11.120)

\displaystyle({\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{t>0}=({\bf x}^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1}(t),{\bf y}^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1}(t),{\bf y}^{\mathrm{aux}}_{2,1}(t))_{t>0},

\displaystyle\bf{\displaystyle z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t)=({z}_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},\qquad{\bf x}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t)=({x}_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},

\displaystyle\bf{\displaystyle y}_{1,1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t)=({y}_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\qquad{\bf y}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t)=({y}_{2,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t))_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}

and its components evolve according to

(11.121)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,i}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg[c_{1}[\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})-x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}^{(1)}g)(x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,i}(t))}\,{\rm d}w(t)

\displaystyle+K_{1}e_{1}\,[y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1,i}(t)-x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle{\rm d}y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1,i}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{1}\,[x^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,i}(t)-y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{1,1,i}(t)]\,{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle y^{\mathrm{aux}}_{2,1,i}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{2,1,i},\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

where

(11.122)

{}\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{i\in[N^{2}]}\frac{x_{i}^{[N^{2}]}+K_{0}y_{i,0}^{[N^{2}]}+K_{1}y_{i,1}^{[N^{2}]}}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\text{ in }L_{2}(\mu).

Let
\mu^{(1)}_{N_{k}}
be the law obtained by periodic continuation of
({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}))_{i\in[N_{k}]}
, and let
\mu^{(1)}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mu^{(1)}_{N_{k}}
be any weak limit point of the sequence
(\mu^{(1)}_{N_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Lemma 11.18
.

[Limiting evolution of auxiliary
1
-block estimator]

Let
\mathcal{L}[({\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(0))]=\mu^{(1)}
. Then the following hold.

1.

For all
t_{1}>0
and
i\in[N_{k}]
,

(11.123)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}(t_{1})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(t_{1})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}\Bigg]=0.

2.

For all
t_{2}>0
,

(11.124)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}\right]=\mathcal{L}[({\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}].

Proof.

Abbreviate

(11.125)

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}t_{1})

\displaystyle=x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1})-\Theta_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}),

\displaystyle\delta_{y_{1},i}^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}t_{1})

\displaystyle=y_{1,1,i}(t_{1})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}),

\displaystyle\delta_{y_{2},i}^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}t_{1})

\displaystyle=y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}).

Extending
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
as a process on
\mathbb{N}_{0}
by periodic continuation, we can construct
({\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
and
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}(N_{k}t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
on one probability space such that

(11.126)

\lim_{k\to\infty}{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})={\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}(0)\quad a.s.

We couple the processes
({\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
and
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
by using the same Brownian motions for both processes. By Itô-calculus we obtain for the coupled process (recall (
11.73
))

(11.127)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(1+K_{0})\left(\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}+K_{1}\left(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}+K_{2}\left(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{2},i}(N_{k}t_{1})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(1+K_{0})\left(\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(0)\right)^{2}+K_{1}\left(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(0)\right)^{2}+K_{2}\left(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{2},i}(0)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2c_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}s)\right)^{2}\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle-2K_{1}e_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}s)-\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(N_{k}s)\right)^{2}\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+2c_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}s)\left(\Theta^{(2)}(t_{2})-\frac{1}{N_{k}^{2}}\sum_{j\in[N_{k}^{2}]}x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{j}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s)\right)\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\left(K_{1}e_{1}+c_{1}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}s)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{j\in[N_{k}]_{i}}x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{j}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)-\bar{\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)\right]\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+2K_{1}e_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(N_{k}s)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\left[\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s)-\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{j\in[N_{k}]_{i}}x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{j}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s)\right]\right]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+2\frac{K_{2}e_{2}}{N_{k}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left[\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(N_{k}s)-\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{j\in[N_{k}]_{i}}x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{j}(N^{2}_{k}s)-\Theta^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{2},i}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+Nt_{1})\right]\Bigg]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+(1+K_{0})^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{F}g\right)(x_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(s))}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s))}\right)^{2}\right]{\rm d}s.

Note that
|\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}|\leq 1
and
|\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}|\leq 1
. Note that the first term tends to
0
by (
11.126
). We show by dominated convergence that also all other positive terms in the right-hand side of (
11.127
) tend to
0
as
k\to\infty
.

For the third term, we can estimate

(11.128)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{c_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{N_{k}^{2}}\sum_{i\in[N_{k}^{2}]}x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{j}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)-\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{c_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{x}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)

\displaystyle-\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{x}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{c_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{x}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)}{1+K_{0}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{x}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{1}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{c_{1}}{1+K_{0}}\Bigg|\frac{\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{x}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{0}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{0}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)+K_{1}\Theta^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1}}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}s)}{1+K_{0}+K_{1}}

\displaystyle-\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})\Bigg|\Bigg].

The first term in (
11.128
) tends to zero by Lemma
11.9
, the second term tends to zero by Lemma
11.16
, while the third term tends to zero by Lemma
11.17
and Lemma
11.5
, which is the third assumption in (
11.101
). Hence the third term in (
11.127
) tends to zero by dominated convergence as
k\to\infty
.

The fourth and fifth term in (
11.127
) tend to zero by Lemma
11.9
and dominated convergence. The sixth term in (
11.127
) tends to zero because the integral is bounded by
t_{1}
and there is a factor
\frac{1}{N_{k}}
in front. To see that the last term in the right-hand side in (
11.127
) tends to zero, recall that the subsequence
N_{k}
is chosen such that

(11.129)

{}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+Nt_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}\right]

exists. Note that

(11.130)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}g)(x_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(s))}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s))}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(\mathcal{F}g)(x_{1}^{\mathrm{aux}}(s))-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}g(x^{[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N^{2}_{k}t_{2}+N_{k}s))\right|\right],

and hence we can apply a similar reasoning as in (
9.97
) to see that (
11.130
) tends to zero as
k\to\infty
. Therefore we obtain

(11.131)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[(1+K_{0})(\Delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{i}(N_{k}t_{1}))^{2}+K_{1}(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{1},i}(N_{k}t_{1}))^{2}+K_{2}(\delta^{(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}_{y_{2},i}(N_{k}t_{1}))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=0.

To prove (
11.124
), note that (
11.131
) implies convergence of the finite-dimensional distributions of
({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
by a similar argument as given below (
9.36
). By Lemma
11.6
we see that the laws of the processes

(11.132)

{}\left(\mathcal{L}\left[({\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}+N_{k}t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}\right]\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}

are tight. Therefore (
11.124
) indeed holds.
∎

Remark 11.19
.

Note that in the proof of Lemma
10.15
we could have proceeded as in the proof of Lemma
11.18
, instead of using the criterion in
[
JM86
, Theorem 3.3.1]
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 11.20
.

[Proof of (
11.102
)]

Under the assumptions in Proposition
11.13
,

(11.133)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v).

Proof.

For ease of notation, we drop the subsequence notation in this proof. Let
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
be any sequence satisfying
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}=\infty
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}/n=0
. For each
t_{n}
, let
\mu^{(1)}_{N,t_{n}}
be the law obtained by periodic continuation of the configuration of
({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n}))_{i\in[N]}
. Recall that, since our state space is compact, the sequence
(\mu^{(1)}_{N,t_{n}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. Let
\mu^{(1)}_{t_{n}}
be any weak limit point of the sequence
(\mu^{(1)}_{N,t_{n}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}[{\bf z}_{1}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(0)]
be the law obtained by periodic continuation of
{\bf\Theta}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n})
. By Lemma
11.6
we know that the sequence

(11.134)

{}\left(\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n}+Nt_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,i\in[N]}\right]\right)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight and hence for each
t_{n}
we can pass to a subsequence such that

(11.135)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-N_{k}t_{n}+N_{k}t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,i\in[N]}\right]

exists. By Lemmas
11.5
–
11.17
, we obtain for all
t_{n}
that

(11.136)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\bar{\Theta}^{(2),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n})=\bar{\Theta}^{(2)}(t_{2})\text{ in probability}.

Then, by (
11.127
) in the proof of Lemma
11.18
, for fixed
t_{n}
and all
i\in[N]
,

(11.137)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-N_{k}t_{n}+N_{k}t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-N_{k}t_{n}+N_{k}t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N_{k}^{2}t_{2}-N_{k}t_{n}+N_{k}t_{n})\right)^{2}\Bigg]=0.

By contradiction we can argue that

(11.138)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n}+Nt_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n}+Nt_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}-Nt_{n}+Nt_{n})\right)^{2}\Bigg]=0.

To see why, suppose that (
11.138
) does not hold. Then for any
\delta>0
we can construct a sequence
(N_{l})_{l>0}
such that, for
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

(11.139)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{l}^{2}]}(N_{l}^{2}t_{2}-N_{l}t_{n}+N_{l}t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{l}^{2}]}(N_{l}^{2}t_{2}-N_{l}t_{n}+N_{l}t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{l}^{2}]}(N_{l}^{2}t_{2}-N_{l}t_{n}+N_{l}t_{n})\right)^{2}\Bigg]>\delta.

However, also the sequence

(11.140)

\left(\mathcal{L}\left[\left({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{l}^{2}]}(N_{l}^{2}t_{2}-N_{l}t_{n}+N_{l}t_{1})\right)_{t_{1}>0,i\in[N]}\right]\right)_{l\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight. Hence we can pass to a further subsequence
(N_{\tilde{l}})_{\tilde{l}\in\mathbb{N}}
for which (
11.137
) holds. But this contradicts (
11.139
). We conclude that (
11.138
) indeed holds. Moreover the argument holds for all
t_{n}
, so that (
11.138
) holds for all
t_{n}
.

Hence for every
t_{n}
there exists a
N_{n}
such that, for all
N\geq N_{n}
,

(11.141)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{k}^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}\Bigg]<\frac{1}{n}.

In particular, we may require that
N_{n}>N_{n-1}
. Setting
N=N_{n}
, we obtain

(11.142)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1+K_{0})\left(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\bar{\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}_{n}]}(N_{n}^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{1}\left(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{1},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{n}^{2}]}(N_{n}^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+K_{2}\left(y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})-\Theta_{y_{2},i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N_{n}^{2}]}(N_{n}^{2}t_{2})\right)^{2}\Bigg]=0.

If we can prove that

(11.143)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})]=\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})},

then we are done. To see why, note that, for all
f\in\mathcal{C}_{b}([0,1]\times[0,1]^{2})
,
f
Lipschitz continuous

(11.144)

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[f({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))]-\mathbb{E}^{\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})}}[f]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[f({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},(1),[N^{2}]}(N^{2}t_{2}))-f({z_{1,i}}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n}))]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}[f({z_{1,i}}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n}))]-\mathbb{E}^{\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})}}[f]\right|.

Therefore if (
11.143
) holds, then for all
\epsilon>0
we can choose
\bar{n}
such that, for all
n>\bar{n}
,

(11.145)

\left|\mathbb{E}[f({z_{1,i}}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n}))]-\mathbb{E}^{\Gamma^{(1)}_{\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})}}[f]\right|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

By (
11.142
) we can find a
\hat{n}>\bar{n}
such that for all
n>\hat{n}

(11.146)

\left|\mathbb{E}[f({\bf\Theta}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux}}(N_{n}^{2}t_{2}))-f({z_{1,i}}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{\hat{n}}))]\right|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Using (
11.143
) and the fact that the Lipschitz functions are dense in
f\in\mathcal{C}_{b}([0,1]\times[0,1]^{2})
, we obtain (
11.133
).

Proof of (
11.143
).

We use that any two systems
({\bf z}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
and
({\bf z}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
evolving according to (
11.28
), and having the same
y_{2,1}
-components and the same
\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})
, can be constructed on one probability space and can coupled by their Brownian motions. We obtain, for a component
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(11.147)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(t_{n})-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(t_{n})|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(t_{n})-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(t_{n})|+K_{2}|y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(t_{n})-y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(t_{n})|]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(0)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(0)|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(0)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(0)|+K_{2}|y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(0)-y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(0)|]

\displaystyle-c\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(s)|]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle-2K_{1}e_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(s)|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(s)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(s)|]

\displaystyle\times 1_{\{{\rm sign}(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(s))\neq{\rm sign}(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},1}(s)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},2}(s))\}}\big]{\rm d}s.

Therefore the difference between these two systems monotonically decreases.

Since the state space
[0,1]\times[0,1]^{2}
is compact, the sequence of laws

(11.148)

{}(\mathcal{L}[{z}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(0)])_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight. Therefore we can find converging subsequences such that

(11.149)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[{z_{i}}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)]=\mu

for some probability measure
\mu
on
[0,1]\times[0,1]^{2}
.

Let
(z^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
be the limiting system evolving according to (
11.28
) and starting from initial distribution
\mu
. By Skorohod’s theorem, we can construct the sequence of limiting systems
((z^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(z^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{1}))_{t_{1}>0}
on one probability space such that

(11.150)

\lim_{k\to\infty}{z}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)=z^{\mathrm{aux},0}(0)\quad a.s.

Use the coupling of Brownian motions to obtain

(11.151)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|

\displaystyle+K_{2}|y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(0)|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(0)|+K_{2}|y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)-y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(0)|]

\displaystyle-c\int_{0}^{t_{n_{k}}}\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(s)|]{\rm d}s

\displaystyle-2K_{1}e_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n_{k}}}\mathbb{E}\big[[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(s)|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(s)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(s)|]

\displaystyle\times 1_{\{{\rm sign}(x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(s)-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(s))\neq{\rm sign}(y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(s)-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(s))\}}\big]{\rm d}s.

Taking the limit
k\to\infty
on both sides of (
11.151
), we obtain

(11.152)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-x_{1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|+K_{1}|y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-y_{1,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|

\displaystyle+K_{2}|y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-y_{2,1,i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})|]=0.

Note that
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}=\infty
implies that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{n_{k}}=\infty
, so
z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}
is the limiting system in (
11.28
) with
\theta
replaced by the random variable
\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})
and
y_{2,1,i}
. Therefore we can condition on
\bar{\Theta}(t_{2})
and
y_{2,1,i}
, and use the assumption in (
11.80
), to obtain

(11.153)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}\left[z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(t_{n_{k}})\right]=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v).

Hence we conclude that

(11.154)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})]=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v).

Equation (
11.154
) holds for all subsequences along which the initial distribution converges,

(11.155)

\lim_{k\to\infty}{z}_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},n_{k}}(0)=z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},0}(0)\quad a.s.

We will show that this implies (
11.143
).

Suppose that

(11.156)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}[z_{i}^{\mathrm{aux},n}(t_{n})]\neq\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\Gamma^{\mathrm{aux},(1)}_{u,y_{2,1}}\,P^{(u,v)}({\rm d}y_{2,1})\,P_{t_{2}}({\rm d}u,{\rm d}v).

Then there exist
